# SIMF Decisions Log

Implementation decisions and assumptions that are not (or not yet) captured in a
controlled document. Each entry is dated and explains the reasoning, per the
project working rules. Newest first.

When a decision is later absorbed into a controlled document, the entry keeps a
note pointing to that document.

| ID | Date | Decision | Reasoning |
|----|------|----------|-----------|
| D-169 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G6 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — SessionQuestion + per-session moderator grant shipped.** PDF §2.7.2 mandates a "session-question moderator" role distinct from D-161's `MobileAppRole.Moderator`; gap doc §4.2 records the naming collision and requires the two never share a label. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.SessionQuestions`):** two new entities. `SessionQuestion(Id, SessionId, SubmittedByUserId, QuestionText ≤1000, Order, IsHidden, IsPushed, PushedAt?, CreatedAt)` — audience-submitted from the mobile app; logical FK on `SubmittedByUserId` (cross-DB to Identity), real FK on `SessionId`. `SessionModerator(SessionId, UserId, AssignedByUserId, AssignedAt)` — composite PK; the per-resource permission grant. Both cascade-delete from Session. **Migration `AddSessionQuestions` on the App DB.** **Services (`SIMF.Application.SessionQuestions.Abstractions` + `SIMF.Infrastructure.SessionQuestions`):** three role-cohesive interfaces — `ISessionQuestionService` (public submission only), `ISessionModerationService` (per-session queue + hide/push/reorder + IsModeratorAsync), `IAdminSessionModeratorService` (admin assign/revoke). Splitting into three rather than one omnibus service keeps each consumer's surface focused (the public endpoint only depends on the submission contract; the moderator endpoints only on the queue contract). Authorization shipped as an in-endpoint helper `SessionModeratorAuth.ResolveAuthorizedUserAsync` (lives in `SIMF.Api`, returns the actor's id when they hold the Administrator role OR have a row in `SessionModerators` for the requested session; null otherwise) — a formal `IAuthorizationRequirement` was considered and rejected as overkill for one policy that's used by exactly four endpoints. **Submission semantics:** the submission endpoint accepts any approved authenticated user (`RequireApprovedAccount`), validates the session exists + is active + is within `[StartUtc - 15m, EndUtc + 1h]` (defining "live" — pre-seating window for audience pre-submits + a post-session Q&A overrun window; tunable later as Options). The §G7 geofence guard is **deferred** — the doc-comment on `SessionQuestionService` records the seam where the geofence check will plug in once G-OI-2 resolves. **Order column race fix (simplify-pass):** the first draft computed `nextOrder = max(Order) + 1` then inserted, a classic TOCTOU race two concurrent submitters would both win. Shipped fix: **every new arrival stores `Order = 0`**; the moderator list sort is `(Order ASC, CreatedAt ASC)`, so unmoderated rows tie at 0 and `CreatedAt` (server `TimeProvider`, monotonic) breaks the tie — natural FIFO with zero race. A moderator reorder writes explicit `Order` values that win. **Reorder full-list contract (simplify-pass):** the original partial-list implementation re-numbered the supplied subset 0..n-1 and left un-listed rows at their old positions, which collided with the new indices. Tightened: the supplied id set must equal the session's full question set (count + content), validated up front, else `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID` / 400. **Hide/unhide is idempotent**; push is one-way (re-pushing keeps the original `PushedAt`). **Endpoints:** public `POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/questions`; moderator `GET /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/moderate`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/{questionId}/hide`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/{questionId}/push`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/reorder`; admin `POST /admin/session-moderators/list`, `POST /admin/session-moderators`, `DELETE /admin/session-moderators/{sessionId}/{userId}`. Moderator endpoints under `RequireApprovedAccount` only — the per-session role check happens inside `HandleAsync` via `SessionModeratorAuth`. Admin endpoints under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`. **Client + BFF:** three admin methods + four moderator methods on `SimfAdminClient` (new `SendSessionsAsync` helper routing through `/api/v1/sessions/`); seven CP BFF passthroughs in `AccountEndpoints`. **CP UI:** `SessionModeratorsList.razor` at `/admin/session-moderators` (admin assign/revoke desk, Administrator-only) + `SessionModerationDesk.razor` at `/sessions/{id}/moderate` (per-session moderator's live Q&A queue). Nav adds `Module.SessionModerators` to the System group. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Sessions.SessionQuestions` + `SIMF.Contracts.Admin.SessionModerators`):** `SubmitSessionQuestionRequest`, `SessionQuestionSubmitted`, `SessionQuestionModeratorRow` (projects submitter display name + email via cross-DB merge), `SetQuestionHiddenRequest`, `ReorderQuestionsRequest`, `AssignSessionModeratorRequest`, `AdminSessionModeratorRow`. **ErrorCodes:** `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID`, `SESSION_QUESTION_NOT_FOUND`, `SESSION_NOT_LIVE_FOR_QUESTIONS`, `SESSION_MODERATOR_NOT_ASSIGNED`, `SESSION_MODERATOR_ALREADY_ASSIGNED`. **AuditEvents:** `SessionQuestion.{Submitted,Hidden,Unhidden,Pushed,Reordered}` + `SessionModerator.{Assigned,Revoked}`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.SessionModerators` + the full `Admin.SessionModerators.*` + `Admin.SessionModeration.*` groups (~32 keys). **Tests:** 13 new — 8 in `SessionQuestionsTests` (submit inside live window returns Order=0; submit outside window returns `SESSION_NOT_LIVE_FOR_QUESTIONS`; empty text → `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID`; moderator queue lists with submitter projection; hide/unhide round-trip + idempotent; push marks pushed + idempotent (`PushedAt` preserved); non-admin non-moderator → 403; granted moderator can read queue without admin role) + 5 in `AdminSessionModeratorsTests` (assign returns row with projection; unknown session → 404 `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`; duplicate → 409 `SESSION_MODERATOR_ALREADY_ASSIGNED`; revoke idempotent; non-admin → 403). **Full suite 356/356 pass.** **Reviews run:** three parallel agents. **Five simplify-pass fixes landed:** (a) Order race fixed by switching to natural FIFO via `CreatedAt` tie-break — see above; (b) reorder partial-list invariant tightened — full-list-only — see above; (c) dropped unused `PushQuestionRequest` contract (the endpoint binds its own route shape, the contract type was never wired); (d) unused `token` parameter removed from test seeder helpers; (e) CP `SessionModerationDesk.razor` hard-coded "Hidden" + "Queued" English literals replaced with `Admin.SessionModeration.State.{Hidden,Queued}` resx keys for AR parity. **What G6 does NOT include yet:** the §G7 geofence guard (still BLOCKED on G-OI-2); the optimistic-concurrency story on Reorder (last-write-wins across concurrent moderators is fine at SIMF scale — one moderator per session is the norm); a "session-now-live" indicator on the session admin grid (small follow-up, not load-bearing); a `dir="auto"` on the question text cell for mixed RTL/LTR submissions (a11y refinement). | Treating the per-session moderator grant as **a permission, not a role** is the load-bearing decision. PDF §2.7.2 + §4.2 spell out that "Moderator" in this context is per-session authority on a Q&A queue — not a global authority on anything else. Inventing a global `Moderator` CP role would have collided with the existing `MobileAppRole.Moderator` (mobile-app content authority introduced by D-161) and forced a rename of one of the two; a per-resource grant on `SessionModerator(SessionId, UserId)` keeps both names in their own bounded contexts. The Administrator-bypass on `SessionModeratorAuth.ResolveAuthorizedUserAsync` matches the same pattern D-148 used for `GateOperator` — admins inherit every per-resource permission so they can step in for an absent moderator. Splitting the three service interfaces (`ISessionQuestionService`, `ISessionModerationService`, `IAdminSessionModeratorService`) by audience instead of bundling into one omnibus matches the R3.5 (D-094) precedent — narrow contracts mean each consumer's constructor signature shows the concerns the service touches. The submission window (`[Start - 15m, End + 1h]`) is opinionated but recoverable: it's hard-coded as `private static readonly TimeSpan`s with doc-comments explaining the rationale (audience pre-seating + post-talk Q&A overrun), and lifting them to Options later is one struct + one DI registration. Refusing to ship Options today is minimum-viable-diff (CLAUDE.md §17) — the values won't change before G7 lands, and G7 will need its own Options group for the geofence anyway. The Order race fix is the most consequential simplify-pass change: switching from `max+1 then insert` to `Order = 0 + tie-break by CreatedAt` is a different data model (the column no longer represents "queue position" in the unmoderated state), and a SOC analyst reading the row at rest will see `Order = 0` for every unmoderated row. Documenting that here so future debugging doesn't waste an hour wondering "why are they all zero?" — they ARE all zero by design, and the moderator's reorder writes the real positions. The reorder full-list contract is the other change worth flagging: a moderator UI that loads N rows then drags-to-reorder will always have the full set in hand, so the constraint costs the UI nothing; a partial-list API call (e.g. "move just these 3 to the top") would have to scan + shift the others manually, which is exactly the invariant the original draft got wrong. The 356/356 test pass rate (was 343/343 pre-G6; +13 from `SessionQuestionsTests` + `AdminSessionModeratorsTests`) covers the live-window check, the moderator-grant gate, the per-action audit trail, the Order-race fix (multi-submit ordering in `Submit_inside_live_window_returns_OK_with_queue_position_zero`), and the cross-DB submitter projection. |
| D-168 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G5 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Public-Relations role + Invitations + VIP list + bulk-notify shipped.** PDF §2.7.3 mandates a public-relations team that manages invitations, views VIP/guest data, tracks attendance + confirmations, and dispatches guest-targeted messages. **Roles (`SIMF.Common.AppRoles`):** new baseline role `PublicRelations` added to `CpRoles` (alongside Administrator, GateOperator). Three new permissions `Invitations.Manage`, `Vips.View`, `Vips.Notify` granted to Administrator + PublicRelations and seeded idempotently by `IdentitySeeder.EnsurePermissionAsync`. **Authorization (`AuthorizationPolicies.PublicRelationsAccess`):** named policy that requires either Administrator OR PublicRelations role — Administrator inherits so an admin can also operate the invitation desk. The role is **NOT** a UserType — PublicRelations is an Admin-typed user with a narrower set of CP grants (matches the D-148 GateOperator pattern). **Open item G-OI-4 (auto-mode resolution):** the PR team shares the existing CP "System" navigation group rather than getting its own layout; documented in `CpNavigation` + the role's XML doc. Splitting later (when the PR team grows past one shared page set) is a pure CP move with no service impact. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.PublicRelations`):** new `Invitation` entity (Id, SentByUserId logical-FK to SimfUser.Id, SentToUserProfileId real-FK to UserProfile.Id after D-167's move, State enum, Notes ≤1000, CreatedAt, RespondedAt?, UpdatedAt?, IsActive). **Enum (`SIMF.Common.Enums.InvitationState`):** brand-new enum Pending=0 / Confirmed=1 / Declined=2; no wire-contract drift to preserve. **EF (`InvitationConfiguration`):** real FK to UserProfile with `OnDelete.Restrict` (matches the rest of App DB's soft-delete policy); three query indexes — `{IsActive, State, CreatedAt}` for SOC sorts, `SentToUserProfileId` for recipient lookups, `SentByUserId` for per-rep filter. Migration `AddInvitations` on the App DB (additive, no schema migration on Identity). **Service (`AdminInvitationService` : `IAdminInvitationService`):** owns invitation CRUD + the VIP-list view + the bulk-notify dispatcher. **VIP discriminator (`SIMF.Common.VipProfileTypes`):** single canonical constant `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` (extracted during the simplify pass — was a per-service literal that would have drifted as VIP-priority lanes get added to the Gate module later). Invitation create dispatches an in-app `NotificationKind.InvitationReceived` row to the recipient (best-effort via `TryDispatchAsync`); state transitions to Confirmed or Declined always update `RespondedAt` even on corrections (Confirmed → Declined re-stamps the latest response time); rolling back to Pending from any settled state is rejected with `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`. NotifyVips fans out one in-app row + one queued email per recipient, drops recipients without an email to in-app only, validates every id against the VIP set (non-VIPs are silently skipped + recorded on `AdminNotifyVipsResult.SkippedProfileIds`). Batch cap 500 ids per request. All writes emit `AuditEvents.Invitation{Created,Updated,StateChanged,Deactivated}` + `Vip.NotificationSent`. **NotificationKind extension:** `InvitationReceived = 30` + `VipBroadcast = 31` (additive, matches D-111's pattern; resx EN + AR + the C# accessor updated). **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/invitations/list`, `GET /admin/invitations/{id}`, `POST /admin/invitations`, `PUT /admin/invitations/{id}`, `DELETE /admin/invitations/{id}`, `POST /admin/vips/list`, `POST /admin/vips/notify` — all under `PublicRelationsAccess` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth`. **Client + BFF:** seven `SimfAdminClient` methods + seven CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** `InvitationsList.razor` (table-based grid with create + edit + cancel actions; edit modal uses a real `SimfSelect<InvitationState>` not a stringly-typed text field — simplify-pass fix) + `VipsList.razor` (selectable rows + bilingual notify modal). Nav adds `Module.Invitations` + `Module.Vips` to the System group. Both pages gate on `[Authorize(Roles = "Administrator,PublicRelations")]`. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Invitations`):** `AdminInvitationSummary`, `AdminInvitationDetail`, `AdminCreateInvitationRequest`, `AdminUpdateInvitationRequest` (opened for inheritance so the endpoint can bind `Id` from the route without a duplicate DTO indirection — simplify-pass fix), `AdminVipSummary`, `AdminNotifyVipsRequest`, `AdminNotifyVipsResult`. **ErrorCodes:** `INVITATION_INVALID`, `INVITATION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVITATION_TARGET_NOT_FOUND`, `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`, `VIP_NOTIFY_EMPTY`, `VIP_NOTIFY_TOO_LARGE`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.Invitations`, `Module.Vips`, the full `Admin.Invitations.*` + `Admin.Vips.*` groups (~50 keys total). **Tests (`AdminInvitationsTests`):** 10 new — create round-trips with recipient projection; create with unknown profile → 400 `INVITATION_TARGET_NOT_FOUND`; update to Confirmed sets RespondedAt + records state-change audit; update from Confirmed back to Pending → 400 `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`; deactivate is idempotent and flips IsActive; VIP list returns only `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` profiles; NotifyVips dispatches valid + skips non-VIP ids; empty UserProfileIds → 400 `VIP_NOTIFY_EMPTY`; non-admin/non-PR caller → 403; PublicRelations role can create invitation end-to-end (proves the role wiring). **Full suite 343/343 pass.** **Reviews run:** three parallel agents (code-reuse, code-quality, efficiency). Seven simplify-pass fixes landed: VIP names extracted to `VipProfileTypes.All`; redundant `UpdateInvitationRequest` wrapper dropped in favor of inheriting from the contract DTO; misleading `RelatedEntityType = nameof(Invitation)` removed from VipBroadcast dispatch (a broadcast is not tied to an Invitation row); `RespondedAt` now updates on every settled-state transition (correction case); CP edit modal swapped stringly-typed `SimfTextField` → typed `SimfSelect<InvitationState>` (typo would silently mis-route); four redundant `Include(ProfileType)` calls dropped (EF Core 6+ join-elision already covers the projection). **What G5 does NOT include yet:** invitation-creation does **not** send an email (the in-app notification is the primary surface; the VIP-broadcast desk is the explicit email path); the recipient picker in the Invitations Add modal currently accepts a raw UserProfile GUID (the next CP polish pass will wire a typeahead against `/admin/vips/list` once the open-item OI-4 layout question is fully resolved); the attendance-tracking surface the PDF §2.7.3 mentions piggybacks on the existing Gate-module scan log (G-OI-4 keeps this in the System group so the PR team uses the same UI). | The semantic justification for treating PublicRelations as a CP RBAC role (not a UserType) is the same one D-148 used for GateOperator: the team is admin-typed users with a narrower CP grant set, not a separate persona on the visitor/other axis. Conflating the two would have forced the JWT minter, the policy stack, and every existing admin endpoint to learn a new UserType discriminator — for zero gain. The `PublicRelationsAccess` policy is deliberately one-of-many (`Administrator OR PublicRelations`) rather than a hard "PublicRelations only" gate because admins must remain able to operate every CP surface — including the invitation desk — for support / on-call coverage. The G-OI-4 "share the System layout" default is the smaller-blast-radius choice; reversing it later is one PR (split layout + move nav items) while reversing the opposite ("invent a new layout group up front") would have meant building UI infrastructure for a team that today has one PR rep at SIMF. The VIP discriminator being a string set `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` (rather than a column on `ProfileType` like `IsVip bool`) preserves the admin-managed lookup: the operator can rename a tier from CP without a code change, and the PR module simply reads whatever names match. The downside is that renaming "VIP" to "Premier" in the CP would break VIP filtering until someone updates `VipProfileTypes.All` — recorded as a known footgun in the constant's XML doc. The brand-new `InvitationState` enum (rather than reusing `AccountState` or `AuditOutcome`) is the right call because the lifecycle has its own vocabulary: Pending → Confirmed/Declined is not the same as Approved/Rejected (account state) or Success/Failure (audit outcome). Three values, additive-only by the freeze rules. The Invitation entity stores `SentByUserId` (the PR rep) as a logical FK to Identity DB because SimfUser lives there post-D-167; we apply the same convention D-167 baked in (cross-DB FKs are logical; same-DB FKs are real). `SentToUserProfileId` is a real FK because UserProfile is now on the same App DB as Invitation. The `OnDelete.Restrict` on that FK matches every other App DB FK — soft-delete is the universal policy, and admins do not hard-delete a UserProfile a PR invitation points at. The `RespondedAt` update rule shipped as **always update on a settled-state transition** rather than the original "only set if null" because a Confirmed → Declined correction is a new response in the PR rep's workflow — the original timestamp would have lied about when the recipient's final word was recorded. The simplify-pass caught this; it's a behavioural change worth calling out separately because it could surprise a SOC analyst looking at the audit trail. Refusing to dispatch a follow-up email on invitation create (the in-app notification is the only surface) keeps the per-invitation cost low — the VIP-broadcast desk is where the PR rep opts in to email. The 500-id batch cap on NotifyVips matches the same scale-bound D-164 used for bulk-approve. The `Include(ProfileType)` drops are a pure EF Core 6+ correctness fix: when the `Select` projects through `p.ProfileType.Name`, the navigation is auto-joined; the explicit `Include` was a no-op that read more columns than the projection needed. Three of four are pure efficiency wins (the fourth was already correct, kept the drop for consistency). The 343/343 test pass rate (was 333/333 pre-G5; +10 from `AdminInvitationsTests`) covers the role-gate, the VIP discriminator, the state-machine, the cross-DB recipient projection, and the bulk-notify dispatch + skip-recording semantic. |
| D-167 | 2026-05-29 | **Bounded-context realignment — `UserProfile`, `ProfileType`, and `Interest` (plus the `UserProfileInterests` M-to-M join) moved from `SimfIdentityDbContext` (Identity DB) to `SimfAppDbContext` (App DB).** Owner-requested ("Move then" on 2026-05-29). The previous arrangement co-located these business entities with ASP.NET Identity for FK convenience, but they are not auth concerns — they are programme/visitor data. After the move, Identity DB owns only the auth surface (`SimfUser`, `SimfRole`, `AspNetUser*`/`AspNetRole*`, `AccountCode`, `RefreshToken`, `SecondFactorToken`, `TotpRecoveryCode`, `Permission`, `RolePermission`); App DB owns business data (programme, gates, profiles, lookups). **Namespace:** the three entity files moved from `SIMF.Domain.IdentityAccess` to a new `SIMF.Domain.Profiles` namespace; the join table moves with them. **Cross-DB FKs:** `UserProfile.UserId → SimfUser.Id` is now a cross-DB **logical FK** (no SQL constraint; service-layer-validated), matching the existing pattern D-153 used for `Speaker.UserProfileId`. **Real same-DB FKs gained** (previously cross-DB / logical): `Speaker.UserProfileId → UserProfile.Id` (Restrict) and `GateProfileTypeAllow.ProfileTypeId → ProfileType.Id` (Restrict). **NOT added:** a real FK on `UserProfile.NationalityId → Country.Id` — even though Country also lives on App DB now, the existing data model allows `NationalityId = 0` on profile stubs (admin-create-user, walk-in pre-fill, test seed fixtures), and a real FK would break that pre-existing behaviour. `NationalityId` stays a logical FK validated by `UserProfileService.ResolveIdAsync`. **Service rewrites:** four services that previously did a single SQL JOIN across `SimfUser` + `UserProfile` and now do App-then-Identity round-trips — `AdminGateService.ListScansAsync` + `ListCurrentlyInsideAsync`, `GateOperatorService.GetMyDailyReportAsync` + `LoadReplayAsync` (new helper `ResolveProfileDisplayNamesAsync`), `QrResolver.ResolveAsync`, `AdminAttendeeService.ListAsync`. Each new pattern batches by user id (PK / unique-index lookup) so total latency stays sub-millisecond. **Save-changes ordering** (post-review correction): `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` saves Identity inside the transaction first; the App-side commit runs **after** the transaction succeeds so an Identity-side rollback drops the profile changes too. `AdminAccountService.RegisterOnsiteAsync`, `CreateAccountAsync`, `ApproveAsync`, `RejectAsync`, and `IdentitySeeder.EnsureProfileTypeAsync` + `RenameProfileTypeIfPresentAsync` all now correctly call `appDbContext.SaveChangesAsync` for App-side rows (a code-review and architect agent both flagged the pre-fix state as silent profile-row data loss). **Migrations:** `MoveProfilesToAppDb` on Identity (drops `UserProfiles`, `Interests`, `ProfileTypes`, `UserProfileInterests`) and on App (creates them). No data-migration step — we are pre-publish per CLAUDE.md §6 freeze rules and dev/test DBs are throwaway. **Tests:** ~20 test files updated (bulk-script + manual sweep): `var appDb = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<SimfAppDbContext>();` added next to every `SimfIdentityDbContext` scope declaration that touched the moved entities; `db.UserProfiles` → `appDb.UserProfiles` (same for ProfileTypes, Interests); `db.RowAudits` → `appDb.RowAudits` in `RowAuditTests` since Interest now audits to the App DB. **Full suite 333/333.** **Reviews run:** code-reviewer + software-architect + database-optimizer agents, three in parallel. The architect + code-reviewer both caught the silent-data-loss pattern (now fixed); the DB optimizer confirmed indexes + storage footprint (~40 MB at 20k users) + the `OnDelete.Restrict` semantics. **Known follow-up (deferred):** the cross-DB write sequence is not atomic — a crash between the Identity commit and the App commit leaves the user state-flipped but the profile un-saved. The user-facing retry is idempotent (`UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` loads-or-creates by `UserId`-unique index), so eventual consistency converges. A full saga/outbox pattern is the right long-term answer; tracked, not blocking. | The semantic argument the owner made is correct: `UserProfile`, `ProfileType`, and `Interest` are programme/visitor data, not authentication concerns. The old arrangement co-located them with Identity because of the single-DB FK to `SimfUser`; after D-157 split the two physical DBs that argument collapsed (`SimfUser.Id ↔ UserProfile.UserId` was already an inter-context relationship enforced in code). Moving the entities aligns the bounded contexts with the bounded responsibilities. Choosing **logical FK** on `UserProfile.UserId → SimfUser.Id` (rather than attempting cross-DB SQL referential integrity which SQL Server does not support) keeps the move pragmatic — the same pattern D-153 already accepted for `Speaker.UserProfileId`, so we are applying an established convention consistently rather than inventing a new one. **Promoting `Speaker.UserProfileId` and `GateProfileTypeAllow.ProfileTypeId` to real same-DB FKs** is a free integrity win — both rows now live in the same physical DB so a real FK costs nothing and prevents the dangling-reference class of bug. **Refusing to add the `UserProfile.NationalityId → Country.Id` real FK** is the most surprising decision and the one that got me to write this paragraph: the existing data model accepts `NationalityId = 0` on profile stubs (admin pre-creates a row before the visitor picks their country), and adding a real FK broke ~20 tests on the first run. Restoring the logical-FK behaviour is minimum-viable-diff per CLAUDE.md §17 — the move's goal was the bounded-context realignment, not a referential-integrity uplift; the latter can ship as a separate decision (with a `NationalityId int?` migration + service-layer pre-fill) when there is owner appetite. **The save-changes ordering correction** is the most important post-review fix: the architect and code-reviewer agents both spotted that the initial commit had `appDbContext.UserProfiles.Add(profile)` followed by only `dbContext.SaveChangesAsync(token)` — the App-side ChangeTracker was tracking the profile but never flushed, which would have shipped silent data loss on visitor onboarding, walk-in registration, admin approve/reject, and ProfileType seeding. The fix (save Identity first inside the transaction, App after the transaction commits) preserves the existing rollback-on-error semantic exercised by `UserProfileRollbackTests` while ensuring the App-side row is persisted on the happy path. The eventual-consistency window between the two commits is the price of a non-MSDTC cross-DB write; documented as a known follow-up rather than papered over. **Choosing to drop and re-create the four tables in one migration pair (no data copy)** is correct only because we are pre-publish — the dev/test DBs are throwaway and there are no production rows to preserve. Post-publish this exact change would require a backup + `INSERT ... SELECT` + `BCP` runbook; the migration's `Down` would need a symmetric script. Recording that boundary explicitly here so a future engineer reading the migration after publish does not assume "EF takes care of it" — it does not. The bulk-PowerShell test refactor was the practical only-realistic approach (touching ~20 files individually would have been hours of manual work); the script's only side-effect was a `identityappDbContext` typo from a too-broad regex which the build caught on the first pass and was fixed in three files. The agent-run for review caught one real bug (silent data loss) and two stale doc comments — well worth the parallel-agent budget. |
| D-166 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G4 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — registration gate + auto-close worker + archive visibility singletons shipped.** PDF §2.3 mandates an admin-toggleable registration open/close gate with an optional scheduled close moment; PDF §2.4 mandates an admin-toggleable archive visibility switch readable by the Flutter app + Website without authentication. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.Operations`):** two singleton entities `RegistrationGate(Id=00..01, IsOpen, AutoCloseUtc?, LastChangedAt, LastChangedByUserId?)` and `ArchiveVisibility(Id=00..02, IsVisible, LastChangedAt, LastChangedByUserId?)`. **EF (`OperationsTogglesConfiguration`):** each table has the singleton row seeded via `HasData` so the row exists from day-one; loader has a defensive fallback that creates the row if `HasData` was dropped. Both rows are persisted on `SimfAppDbContext`. Migration `AddOperationsToggles` on the App DB (additive, D-135 freeze-lift carries forward). **Service (`OperationsToggleService` : `IOperationsToggleService`):** read + admin-write surface for both toggles. `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` is the hot-path call sign-up makes on every request: it returns false when `IsOpen=false` OR `AutoCloseUtc <= now` (no race vs. the worker — the service-side check enforces the rule even before the worker flips IsOpen). Updates write only when something actually changed (idempotent re-saves do not bump LastChangedAt or write an audit row). Audit events `RegistrationGate.Updated`, `RegistrationGate.AutoClosed`, `ArchiveVisibility.Updated`, `SignUp.RejectedRegistrationClosed`. **Background worker (`RegistrationGateAutoCloseWorker` : `BackgroundService`):** polls once per minute; flips `IsOpen=false` the first tick after `AutoCloseUtc` passes; writes the `RegistrationGate.AutoClosed` audit row with `ActorUserId=null` (the worker is not a person). The worker does not race admins: it writes only when IsOpen is still true AND AutoCloseUtc ≤ now; a concurrent admin re-open that clears AutoCloseUtc simply leaves the worker with nothing to flip. **Sign-up wiring:** `RegistrationService` gained an `IOperationsToggleService` dependency and consults the gate **before** the email-exists check — closed gate returns 403 `REGISTRATION_CLOSED`, no user row, no email enqueued. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/v1/admin/registration-gate` + `PUT /api/v1/admin/registration-gate` (admin) under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth` policy; `GET /api/v1/archive/visibility` (**AllowAnonymous** — Flutter + Website need to read this before authenticating); `GET /api/v1/admin/archive/visibility` + `PUT /api/v1/admin/archive/visibility` (admin). **Client + BFF:** four `SimfAdminClient` methods + four CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** single `/admin/operations` page (Razor) rendering both toggles in one place — IsOpen checkbox + datetime-local AutoCloseUtc input for the gate, IsVisible checkbox for the archive, both with LastChangedAt readouts and separate Save buttons. `CpNavigation.Module.OperationsToggles` added to the System group. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Operations`):** `RegistrationGateState`, `UpdateRegistrationGateRequest`, `ArchiveVisibilityState`, `UpdateArchiveVisibilityRequest`. **ErrorCodes:** `REGISTRATION_CLOSED`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.OperationsToggles` + the full `Admin.Operations.*` group (~22 keys). **Tests (`OperationsTogglesTests`):** 7 new — admin GET returns seeded open state; closing the gate makes sign-up return 403 REGISTRATION_CLOSED; sign-up succeeds when the gate is open (regression guard); AutoCloseUtc in the past blocks sign-up even with IsOpen=true (service-side check enforces the rule before the worker ticks); public `GET /archive/visibility` requires no auth; admin can toggle archive visibility and the public endpoint reflects it; non-admin caller cannot PUT either toggle. **What G4 does NOT include yet:** the AutoCloseUtc-passed worker-flip behaviour is not directly exercised by a test (the worker runs on a 1-minute poll inside the host; `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` enforces the rule independently, so the user-visible behaviour is already covered). The CP form does not surface a "force-flip the worker now" button — the AutoCloseUtc setter is already the user-facing affordance. | Modelling the two singletons as separate tables (rather than a generic key/value `OperationsConfig` table) keeps the schema strongly typed: `AutoCloseUtc` is a real `datetimeoffset null` column, not a stringified blob inside a value column. The cost is two trivial tables vs. one; the gain is that future additions (e.g. a `MaxConcurrentSignUpsPerMinute int` knob) get their own column rather than another magic key. The singleton Id strategy (`Guid.Parse("00..01")` / `00..02`) keeps the row addressable from EF `HasData` seeding without inventing a synthetic enum-backed PK; admins never see the Id. The hot-path `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` is deliberately a single AsNoTracking query (no caching layer) because (a) sign-up is rate-limited by middleware ahead of this, so the QPS is bounded; (b) caching would create a window where a closed gate still accepts a few sign-ups, which is exactly the surprise the gate exists to prevent; (c) the SqlServer plan for a single-row PK lookup is sub-millisecond. The AutoCloseUtc enforcement is **double-belt**: the service-side check fails the sign-up the moment the moment passes, and the worker flips the persistent `IsOpen` row within a minute so the CP grid reflects the new state. Without the service-side check, a 1-minute window after AutoCloseUtc would still accept sign-ups; without the worker, the persistent `IsOpen` would lie until an admin refreshed the page. Both layers are intentional. The "no audit row when nothing changed" rule prevents an admin who hits Save twice from generating a noise audit trail; idempotency matches the broader audit-log discipline. Sign-up rejection is **403 not 503** because the gate is a deliberate policy state, not an outage — 503 would invite retry, which is wrong; 403 with a typed code lets the Flutter app render the right "registration is closed" copy without retrying. The public `/archive/visibility` endpoint is anonymous on purpose: the Flutter app + Website renders the public landing page **before** the user signs in, and gating the visibility decision behind auth would force every public surface to display the archive optimistically and then hide it after sign-in, which is worse UX than asking unauthenticated. Bundling both toggles on a single `/admin/operations` page (rather than two separate pages) matches the small surface area — both are single-row config knobs, and forcing two nav clicks for two checkboxes would be ceremony. The PageColor / icon decisions on the nav are deferred — the System group already has 15 items and adding two more nav entries would push it past the visible-without-scroll budget on a 1080p laptop. |
| D-165 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G3 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Sessions module shipped end-to-end + CP `Module.Sessions` flipped from stub to real route.** PDF §2.9 mandates "الجلسات" as a first-class concept distinct from "المحاور" (Themes). **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.Programme`):** `Session` entity (Id, Code 2–16 unique, Title/TitleArabic ≤256, Description/DescriptionArabic ≤2048, real-FK `HallId` + `Hall` nav, StartUtc/EndUtc DateTimeOffset, optional `CapacityOverride int?`, IsActive, CreatedAt/UpdatedAt) + two explicit join entities `SessionSpeaker` (composite PK SessionId+SpeakerId + DisplayOrder for primary/supporting ordering) and `SessionTheme` (composite PK SessionId+ThemeId). **EF (`SessionConfiguration` + `SessionSpeakerConfiguration` + `SessionThemeConfiguration` under `Persistence/Configurations/App`):** real FK to Hall with `OnDelete.Restrict` (matches the existing soft-delete policy — admins deactivate halls, never hard-delete a hall a session points at), join sides cascade from Session, restrict from Speaker/Theme. Two query indexes the agenda screen + operator views ride: `{IsActive, StartUtc}` and `{HallId, StartUtc}`. **Migration `AddSessions` on `SimfAppDbContext`** — additive, single migration, no `__EFMigrationsHistory` divergence (carries D-135 freeze-lift). **Service (`AdminSessionService` : `IAdminSessionService`):** List with search across Code/Title/TitleArabic + `isActive` and `hallId` filters; Get returns the join graph fully populated; Create/Update validate (Code 2–16 unique, Title 1–256 EN+AR, end > start, capacity ≥ 0 or null), resolve Hall (must exist + IsActive — else `SessionHallNotFound`/400), check all SpeakerIds exist + IsActive (else `SessionSpeakerNotFound`/400; duplicate speaker in same payload → `SessionInvalid`/400), check all ThemeIds exist + IsActive (else `SessionThemeNotFound`/400). Duplicate code on Create or rename-to-existing on Update → `SessionCodeDuplicate`/409. Update replaces the speaker + theme link sets atomically. Deactivate is idempotent. All three writes emit `AuditEvents.Session{Created,Updated,Deactivated}`. **Effective capacity rule (PDF §2.9):** `EffectiveCapacity = CapacityOverride ?? Hall.Capacity` — most sessions inherit; only reconfigured rooms override. **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/sessions/list`, `GET /admin/sessions/{id}`, `POST /admin/sessions`, `PUT /admin/sessions/{id}`, `DELETE /admin/sessions/{id}` under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth` policy. **Client + BFF:** `SimfAdminClient.{List,Get,Create,Update,Deactivate}SessionAsync` + five CP BFF passthroughs in `AccountEndpoints`. **CP UI:** `SessionsList.razor` (multiselect grid with Code/Title/TitleArabic/Hall/StartUtc/EndUtc/Capacity/Status columns + Details modal) + `SessionForm.razor` (Hall picker lazily loaded from `/admin/halls/list`, Speaker multi-picker with up/down/remove reorder buttons that renumber DisplayOrder, Theme multi-picker as chips, datetime-local inputs for StartUtc/EndUtc treated as UTC). `CpNavigation.Module.Sessions` flipped from `/m/sessions` IsStub to real `/admin/sessions`. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Sessions`):** `AdminSessionSummary`, `AdminSessionDetail`, `AdminSessionSpeakerEntry(SpeakerId, Name, NameArabic, DisplayOrder)`, `AdminCreateSessionRequest`, `AdminUpdateSessionRequest`. Hall's seat count is exposed as `HallCapacity` (matches the Hall entity's actual `Capacity` field name). **ErrorCodes:** `SessionInvalid`, `SessionNotFound`, `SessionCodeDuplicate`, `SessionInvalidTimeWindow`, `SessionHallNotFound`, `SessionSpeakerNotFound`, `SessionThemeNotFound`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for the full `Admin.Sessions.*` group (≈70 keys). **Tests (`AdminSessionsTests`):** 8 new — create with hall + default capacity inherits; create with capacity override wins over hall capacity; unknown hall → 400 `SessionHallNotFound`; end ≤ start → 400 `SessionInvalidTimeWindow`; duplicate code → 409 `SessionCodeDuplicate`; speakers + themes persist and round-trip in detail (with DisplayOrder); deactivate is idempotent + flips IsActive; non-admin → 403. **Full suite 326/326 pass.** **What G3 does NOT include yet:** the public session page on the Website + Flutter agenda binding to the new Session table (separate work — the admin CRUD is the prerequisite); the session-question moderator workflow (lands as G6 once `SessionQuestion` ships). | Sessions are the highest-impact PDF §2.9 gap because the Mockup's entire agenda screen, the booking module, and the future SessionQuestion thread all bind to this entity — shipping the admin CRUD first means every later increment binds to a real table rather than a stub. Storing the join sets as **explicit** entities (SessionSpeaker, SessionTheme) instead of implicit M-to-M follows the D-148 GateProfileTypeAllow precedent: the join row carries metadata (DisplayOrder for primary/supporting speakers) and the explicit type makes future surface additions (e.g. "is this speaker the chair?" boolean) cheap. The capacity override defaults to NULL precisely because the PDF §2.9 case — "most sessions inherit the hall, a few reconfigured rooms override" — would otherwise pollute every row with a duplicate of the Hall capacity. The effective-capacity computation lives in the service projection rather than the entity to keep the entity dumb and the rule in one place. Choosing `OnDelete.Restrict` for the Hall FK matches the existing soft-delete policy across the rest of the programme module (Speaker→Country was also Restrict in D-153) — admins deactivate, they never hard-delete a referenced row. The `SessionSpeakerNotFound` / `SessionThemeNotFound` error codes are deliberately distinct from `SessionInvalid` because the front-end needs to know whether to re-load the speaker / theme picker (the row was deactivated mid-edit) or surface a generic validation error. The CP form's Up/Down DisplayOrder buttons (instead of drag-and-drop) match the SimfDataGrid + SimfSelect language already used across the CP — drag-and-drop would have required a new Component and JS interop hook just to reorder a list that typically holds 1–4 speakers. The Theme picker is a flat multi-select rather than a primary-vs-secondary split because the PDF §2.9 only mentions "themes belong to a session" — promoting one as primary is not a stated requirement, and we can add it as an additive column later without breaking the wire. Flipping `Module.Sessions` from the `/m/sessions` stub route to the real `/admin/sessions` route in this same commit closes the "navigation lies about what exists" debt that piles up when stub routes survive past the moment their destination is real. |
| D-164 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G2 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — bulk-approve "Select All" affordance shipped + Moderator-naming clarification documented.** PDF §2.7.1 mandates a "Select all" bulk-approve for the security team. **Service:** `IAdminUserApprovalService` gains `BulkApproveVisitorsAsync` and `BulkApproveOthersAsync`, both returning a typed `AdminBulkApprovalResponse(Approved, Skipped, Failures[])`. Per-subject failures are captured (with the typed `ReasonCode`, English + Arabic message) and do not block the rest of the batch. Server clamps the batch at 500 distinct ids per request to bound the transaction window. **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/visitors/bulk-approve` and `POST /api/v1/admin/others/bulk-approve` under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, rate-limited under the existing `auth` policy. Empty `Ids[]` is `AdminBulkActionInvalid`/400. **Client + BFF:** new `SimfAdminClient.BulkApprove{Visitors,Others}Async` methods + two CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** `SimfDataGrid` gains a new `OnApproveSelected` `EventCallback<IReadOnlyList<TItem>>` parameter + `ApproveSelectedLabel` string parameter. Toolbar renders an "Approve selected" button when the parent supplies the handler, enabled only when ≥ 1 row on the current page is selected. `PendingVisitors.razor` and `PendingOthers.razor` wire `OnApproveSelected="OnBulkApproveAsync"` with localised labels. Toast surfaces `Approved {N} / Skipped {M}`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Admin.Pending.Action.BulkApprove` and `Admin.Pending.BulkApproved.Toast`. **Tests:** `AdminBulkApprovalTests` — 4 new (3-subject happy path → all flip to Approved with FK verified in DB; one good + one unknown id → 1 approved / 1 failure with the unknown id surfaced in `Failures[0].UserId`; empty array → 400 `AdminBulkActionInvalid`; non-admin → 403). **Doc:** MAA-001 §8.1.1 added — disambiguates `MobileAppRole.Moderator` (mobile-app content authority, global JWT claim) from the **session-question moderator** (per-session permission, لقب المحاور in the PDF, lands in gap-plan phase G3). Both authorities can coexist on one user; the Flutter app must not infer one from the other. **Full suite 318/318 pass.** **What G2 does NOT include yet:** the session-question moderator workflow itself — the `Session` entity does not exist yet, and the per-session permission grant requires it. That work is scheduled in phase G3 alongside the Sessions module. | The bulk-approve service deliberately processes each subject in its own atomic step (rather than one big transaction) so a single bad row does not roll back the rest of the batch — a 500-row "Select All" with three already-rejected subjects should still approve the 497 valid ones. Returning the per-subject failures by id + email + bilingual reason matches the audit-log pattern (one row per subject) and gives the CP enough information to render inline errors next to the rows that did not flip. Adding `OnApproveSelected` to `SimfDataGrid` as a new event (rather than reusing `CustomToolbar`) keeps the toolbar layout consistent across grids — the bulk-approve button sits next to the bulk-delete button by design. The change is additive: every existing grid that doesn't pass the handler renders identically. The MAA-001 §8.1.1 callout is the most important defensive doc-write of this commit: the PDF and D-161 use the same English label "Moderator" for two genuinely different authorities, and a future engineer reading either document alone would silently conflate them. The doc table forces the disambiguation up to the surface. Deliberately deferring the session-question moderator implementation to G3 honours `CLAUDE.md` §17 minimum-viable-diff — that work needs the `Session` entity which does not yet exist, and inventing it just to host one role grant would be the wrong order. The 500-id batch cap is sized for the SIMF event scale (a typical pending queue runs in the dozens to low hundreds, not thousands) and bounds the transaction window without artificially capping the UX. |

Both are display categories with no operational authority — a sponsor representative is not automatically a gate operator. **Docs:** `SIMF-CPD-001` table clarifies that **Theme** = pillar (editorial grouping + colour token) and **Session** = scheduled run-of-show talk (HallId + time window + speakers); the two coexist and the PDF §2.9 "Agenda → Sessions" rename applies on public surfaces only (CP labels were already "Sessions"). `SIMF-FDS-002` OI-6 is updated to "partially closed" (Other-tier seeds added; Visitor-tier still owner-pick) and OI-9 is updated to "mandated by D-162, scheduled phase G2". **Tests:** all 314 pass under the new column. No new tests added — the column is read/write pass-through, the existing UpsertUserProfileRequest round-trip exercises the path. **Files touched:** 13 code, 4 resx (2 EN + 2 AR), 2 docs, 1 decisions-log. | The JobTitle column is the smallest schema delta that closes a PDF requirement (§2.6 "المسمى الوظيفي اختياري") end-to-end across every read/write seam — domain, EF, contract, service, validator, UI, resx. Treating it as a single G1 sub-phase rather than parking it as a deferred enhancement matches §17 minimum-viable-diff: the column is shipping anyway; threading it now is cheaper than reopening every layer later. Seeding `Media` and `Sponsor` as canonical Other-tier types matches the PDF §2.5 owner-quoted set (`إعلامي`, `راعي`) — these are the only two PDF-named Other subtypes beyond `Staff`, and they ship with `MobileAppRole=None` precisely because the PDF assigns them no operational authority. The CPD-001 Theme-vs-Session clarification preempts a real bug class — a future engineer reading "Theme" and "Session" as synonyms would corrupt the agenda-rename work in phase G3. Updating the two FDS-002 open items in this commit (rather than waiting until G2 actually ships the bulk-approve) keeps the canonical OI table in sync with the gap-doc plan; the alternative — leaving OI-9 as "decide whether" while the gap doc says "scheduled" — would leave the truth split across two documents. The G1 commit deliberately does not start G2 (bulk-approve) or G3 (Sessions module) work, even though both are next in the gap doc, because they are bigger ships with their own decision logs and should not be conflated with the G1 quick-wins commit. |
| D-162 | 2026-05-29 | **The stakeholder requirements PDF at `D:\SIMF\System\15-04-2024\متطلبات التطبيق والموقع الإلكتروني للملتقى + ملاحظات على Mockup.pdf` is promoted to authoritative for forward planning.** Owner direction on 2026-05-29 after the PDF was read into the conversation. The remainder of the `15-04-2024` folder stays superseded per the original `SIMF-OLD-DRAFT-CONFLICTS.md` ruling. Where this PDF conflicts with a prior `docs/SIMF-*` controlled document, the PDF wins for the affected item and the controlled document is updated in the sprint that closes the gap (not retroactively). The full requirements extraction, gap analysis against the current `feature/login-api` baseline, conflict log, and 11-phase closure plan (G1 → G11) is recorded in `docs/SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap.md`. **What this immediately changes:** (a) `FDS-002 OI-6` (default `ProfileTypes` seed set) is partially closed — Other-tier adds `Media (إعلامي)` and `Sponsor (راعي)`; Visitor-tier remains owner-pick. (d) The PDF references Mockup pages 7 / 21 / 26 / 27 / 39 for action — the Mockup deliverable from the external UI/UX designer is a hard gate on phase G11. **No code, no migration, no contract change shipped with this entry — gap document only.** | Promoting one specific PDF inside an otherwise-superseded folder is the smallest authority change that captures the stakeholder direction without retroactively reactivating the rest of the early-draft material. The alternative — declaring the whole `15-04-2024` folder authoritative — would silently undo every decision (D-001 → D-161) that resolved earlier conflicts in that folder. The narrower exception preserves the work already shipped while letting the new direction land cleanly. Writing the gap document first (and the closure plan into 11 explicit phases) before touching code is exactly the "Plan first, then STOP for approval" discipline from `CLAUDE.md` §1.4 and §11: the phases are presented in priority order with effort estimates, but no phase is approved until the owner picks it. The two FDS-002 OI updates (OI-6 partial, OI-9 mandated) reflect what changed in the open-items list; the FDS-002 controlled document itself does not get an Amendment C in this entry because the gap document is the canonical record of which items moved and where. The naming collision between PDF §2.7.2 المحاور and `MobileAppRole.Moderator` is the highest-risk overlap surfaced by this read-through: a fresh engineer reading the code would assume the two refer to the same role and silently grant session-question moderation authority through the JWT claim, which is not what either concept means. Phase G2 captures the rename plan explicitly so this does not get forgotten. |
| D-161 | 2026-05-29 | **Other-ProfileType → mobile-role mapping shipped as data — `ProfileType.MobileAppRole` admin-curated column + JWT `mobile_app_role` claim. Closes the first of the two §8.5 / staff-app blocking decisions.** **Enum (`SIMF.Common.Enums.MobileAppRole`)** — None (0) / Visitor (1) / Staff (2) / Moderator (3). Numeric values are stable, persisted on `ProfileType.MobileAppRole`, and travel as the stringly-typed `mobile_app_role` JWT claim. **Domain column** — `ProfileType.MobileAppRole MobileAppRole` (non-nullable, defaults to `None`). EF persists as the enum-name string with `HasDefaultValue(MobileAppRole.None)` so the migration backfills any existing rows safely. **Migration** — `AddProfileTypeMobileAppRole` on `SimfIdentityDbContext` adds one nvarchar(16) column with default `None`. **Seeder default** — `Staff` (Other) is seeded with `MobileAppRole.Staff` out of the box; the other operational types (Volunteer / Programme Coordinator / Operations Lead) are admin-seeded at runtime per event, not hardcoded — keeps the "data, not code" promise. **JWT pipeline** — `IJwtTokenService.CreateAccessToken` gained a `MobileAppRole` parameter; both callers (`SignInService.IssueTokensAsync`, `SessionService.RefreshAsync`) resolve it via the new `IUserProfileService.ResolveMobileAppRoleAsync(userId, ct)`. Resolver logic: `Visitor → MobileAppRole.Visitor` (from UserType, not from a ProfileType row); `Admin → MobileAppRole.None`; `Other → profile.ProfileType.MobileAppRole` (or `None` when no profile type assigned). The Flutter app reads the claim once at sign-in; no extra round-trip to a lookup endpoint. **Admin CRUD** — `AdminCreateProfileTypeRequest` / `AdminUpdateProfileTypeRequest` gained an optional `MobileAppRole` string field (defaults to `None`). `AdminProfileTypeSummary` exposes it back. `AdminProfileTypeCommandService.ParseMobileAppRole` rejects unknown values and explicit `Visitor` (which is resolved from UserType, never from a ProfileType row) with a typed 400. The endpoint `UpdateAdminProfileTypeRouteRequest` forwards the new field to the service request DTO. **Tests** — `MobileAppRoleTests` (6 new): CRUD round-trip carries the value; default-None when omitted; explicit `Visitor` rejected 400; update mutates the value; Visitor sign-in JWT carries `mobile_app_role=Visitor`; Admin sign-in JWT carries `mobile_app_role=None`. **Deferred to a follow-up commit:** the CP form / picker for editing `MobileAppRole` (admin can already edit it via the API; the picker UI is a separate concern), the resx labels, and the SIMF-FDS-002 §8.5 doc closure note that references this entry. **Full suite 314/314.** | Choosing "data, not code" for the mapping is the single most important decision here: the alternative — hardcoding a per-ProfileType-name switch in the JWT issuer — would have shipped a list that goes stale the first time an admin renames a profile type or adds a new operational one. The cost (one nvarchar column + one admin field) is trivial; the saved future bug is real ("after I created the Lead Coordinator ProfileType last week, why don't they have Moderator authority in the app?"). Rejecting `Visitor` as a settable value on a ProfileType row is honest about the data model: Visitor authority is computed from `UserType`, not assigned per ProfileType. Letting it through the API would have created two sources of truth for the same value. The JWT claim plumbing went through `IUserProfileService` rather than a new `IMobileAppRoleResolver` because the resolver method is exactly one DB query and the interface already owns "things you ask about a user's profile" — adding a third abstraction for one method violates §17 minimum-viable-diff. The CP-UI / resx deferral is the same calibration: admins can curate via API right now (Postman, curl, or the eventual CP form), and shipping the form requires a meaningful UI design pass that's not on the staff-app critical path. The data layer is the bottleneck; the picker is convenience. Numeric stability of the enum values matters because we'll likely persist them in claim caches and downstream-mobile event-bus envelopes; ordering them None=0 → Moderator=3 lets `MobileAppRole > Staff` be a meaningful authority comparison if the Flutter app ever needs to express "this surface requires ≥ Moderator." Setting `Visitor = 1` between `None` and `Staff` is deliberate: an unauthenticated request is Guest (= 0 in the Flutter app's own enum) and a signed-in but un-elevated user is Visitor; both are below operational tiers. |
| D-160 | 2026-05-29 | **`POST /api/v1/gates/{gateId}/visitors/list` — cursor-paged staff-app view of scans at a single gate, backed by the D-158 snapshot columns. Approved wire contract.** Closes the second of the two open §8.5 / staff-app blocking decisions from the owner. **Request shape:** opaque base64-JSON `Cursor`, client-hint `PageSize` (server-clamped 1..200, default 50), optional `Direction` / `Outcome` / `SinceUtc` / `UntilUtc` filters. `Outcome` defaults to `Allowed` when null — the "who's at this gate right now" view is the staff app's primary use case, so the default matches it. **Response shape:** `Items` (snapshot-backed identity fields — `DisplayName`, `ProfileTypeName`, plus `QrIdAtScan`, `UserProfileId`, `Direction`, `Outcome`, `DenialReasonCode`), opaque `NextCursor` (null when no more), `AsOfUtc` server clock for clock-skew detection on the polling staff app. **Cursor strategy:** single `lastSeenScanId` encoded as base64 JSON `{"lastId":N}`; the JSON wrapper means the wire format can grow without breaking older clients. WHERE-clause is `Id > cursor` on the existing `IX_GateScan_UserProfile_ScannedAt` ordering — bigint identity PK is monotonic, no fragmentation under inserts. **Refresh behaviour:** same endpoint, same cursor — staff app polls every ~10s with the previous response's `NextCursor`; server returns only items with `Id > lastSeenScanId`. No separate "delta" endpoint, no WebSocket today. **Snapshot fields, not JOIN.** `DisplayName` and `ProfileTypeName` come straight from D-158's `GateScan.ScannedDisplayName` / `ScannedProfileTypeName` columns; the endpoint never reaches across to `SIMF_Identity`. Zero cross-DB latency, zero stale-data risk under cross-DB drift. **Authorization:** `GatesViewOwnReports` policy (same as `GET /gates/my-reports/today`); the service-layer assignment check returns `GateNotFound`/404 for an unknown gate and `NotAssigned`/403 for a known gate the caller doesn't operate — same shape as `POST /scans` already uses. **PII excluded by design:** no email, no national-id, no phone, no avatar URL in the list view — staff at a gate confirming "Ahmad walked in" doesn't need those fields, and dragging them through every poll would 10x the response size for no UX gain. A per-scan detail endpoint (out of scope here) can return the richer envelope when an operator taps a row. **Malformed-cursor handling:** treated as no cursor, first page returned (rather than 400). Friendlier under the operator-poll loop — a corrupted cursor recovers automatically instead of failing every subsequent poll until the app restarts. **Tests:** 7 new (`GateVisitorsListTests`) covering snapshot fields populated, cursor paging end-to-end (5 scans / pageSize 2 → 3 pages), default `Allowed` filter excludes denied, explicit `Denied` returns only denied, operator-not-assigned 403, unknown-gate 404, malformed cursor → first page. **Full suite 308/308.** | The endpoint design is a direct application of every prior decision in this arc: D-148 (the gate engine), D-149 (the operator surface), D-157 (the split DBs), D-158 (the snapshot columns). The snapshot pattern was specifically motivated by this exact endpoint — without it, the list view would have to JOIN across `SIMF_App.GateScans` and `SIMF_Identity.UserProfiles` per row, which under physical separation is two queries plus an in-process merge, scaling badly. The snapshot makes the read a single-table query against an already-indexed column. Cursor-over-offset is non-negotiable for an append-only log polled continuously: offset pagination breaks under concurrent inserts (rows shift between pages), but `lastSeenScanId` is stable under any number of new inserts. The JSON-wrapped cursor (vs a bare integer) buys forward compatibility for ~zero cost: a future increment that needs to also encode "scanned-by user" or "filter snapshot" can add fields without breaking older clients that only know `lastId`. The Allowed-as-default-outcome is a usage-shaped decision, not a generic one — "show me everyone inside" is the primary need; "show me everyone refused entry" is the secondary need (and gets its own `Outcome=Denied` parameter); "show me both" is the rare reconciliation need (and gets a client-side merge). Excluding PII from the list shape is the same calibration: forensic review wants the richer envelope, operational glance wants display-name + outcome + time. Different views, different shapes. The 403-vs-404 split for "wrong gate" vs "unknown gate" mirrors the `POST /scans` decision (D-148): leaking "gate X exists, you're not assigned" is safer than leaking "gate X does not exist" via timing differences, and the existing failure-rate circuit + audit log already track both cases at the engine layer. The malformed-cursor-is-not-fatal decision is operations ergonomics: a polling client that crashes on its first malformed response will keep crashing every 10s forever; one that quietly resets to first page recovers itself. |

Removing the redundant `HasIndex(speaker.CountryId)` matches EF's auto-index-on-FK behaviour; carrying the manual line would mean the migration tries to create an index that already exists (or worse, two indexes on the same column). The two-explicit-OnDelete-passes pattern (Restrict here, SetNull elsewhere) is the right default for SIMF: data-loss-on-delete is reversible-via-restore at best and silent-corruption at worst, so the DB blocks it and forces the admin to deactivate explicitly. Closing the freeze-removal arc here: D-110 frozen → D-151 freeze removed → D-152/153 cross-context FKs (logical) → D-157 split DBs → D-158 snapshot pattern → D-159 same-DB FK promoted to real. The two columns serve different questions and live together. | The snapshot pattern is the audit-design industry default for cross-domain references (cf. event sourcing, financial audit logs, healthcare HIPAA logs): **always store both the FK and a name snapshot**, because the FK answers "who is this person (today)" and the snapshot answers "who were they at the moment of the action." For SIMF the cross-DB physical split (D-157) makes the FK answer cross-DB and therefore loose — but that doesn't change the snapshot's purpose. Even under one shared DB the snapshot would still be the right call for audit-grade logs. The JWT claim is a frozen-at-token-issue snapshot too — which is fine for our retention window because tokens expire in 30 minutes per JwtOptions and a name change forces a re-login (security_stamp invalidation). The decision to make `SubjectDisplayName` on `AuditEntry` optional rather than required came from §17 minimum-viable-diff: requiring it would force ~30 audit-write call sites to look up the name before logging, and most of those call sites already have an email + user-id, so the column is rarely null-and-also-meaningfully-missing anyway. Build batches into one commit. **DB-side:** `UserProfile.NationalityId int NOT NULL` (logical FK to `SIMF.Domain.Common.Country.Id`, indexed); **no DB FK constraint** — the two rows live in different DbContexts (`SimfIdentityDbContext` for the profile, `SimfAppDbContext` for the country) sharing one physical database with separate migration ledgers, and EF cannot express a cross-ledger constraint without violating the D-109 invariant that each context owns its own `__EFMigrationsHistory_*` table; the FK is enforced in application code at write time instead. **Wire contract preserved:** `UserProfileResponse.NationalityCode`, `UpsertUserProfileRequest.NationalityCode`, `AdminWalkInRegistrationRequest.NationalityCode`, `AdminUserProfileView.NationalityCode`, `PendingProfileResponse.NationalityCode` all stay as ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 strings — the wire surface is ergonomic and ISO-aware; the int Id is an implementation detail. Cross-context lookup is a direct `SimfAppDbContext` injection — no new `ICountryLookup` abstraction since two cheap single-row index queries don't earn one. **Validator simplified:** `UpsertUserProfileRequestValidator` and `AdminWalkInRegistrationRequestValidator` drop the static-list existence check (`Countries.IsKnown`); FluentValidation is sync and the lookup table is now cross-context, so existence enforcement moves to the service layer (defence-in-depth was the only reason for the duplicate check anyway). **`ProfileCountriesEndpoint`** now reads live `appDb.Countries` filtered by `IsActive` ordered by `DisplayOrder` then `NameEn`, replacing the static curated list. **`SIMF.Common.Countries` (62-entry static class) deleted** — the picker is now admin-curated via the Country CRUD added in Phase A. **Identity migration regenerated as a single `InitialCreate`** (`20260529115746_InitialCreate.cs`) — the prior `InitialCreate` was deleted along with its `Designer.cs` + `SimfIdentityDbContextModelSnapshot.cs`; the dev DB had been dropped by the owner earlier in this session, so a clean single-migration history is preferable to an additive `ChangeNationalityCodeToNationalityId` that would carry a `MigrationBuilder.AlterColumn` for a column that never shipped to production. App-context migrations chain (`InitialCreate_App` → `AddThemes` → `AddHalls` → `AddGates` → `AddSpeakers` → `AddCountries`) unchanged. **Test impact:** only `GateScanTests.cs` needed touching — it creates a `UserProfile` entity directly (`NationalityCode = "SA"` → `NationalityId = 682`, the ISO 3166-1 numeric for Saudi Arabia from the AddCountries seed). Every other test sends DTOs over the wire, where the contract is unchanged. **Full suite 294/294 pass.** **Files touched:** 7 source files + 1 test file modified, 4 files deleted (3 old Identity migration files + `SIMF.Common/Countries.cs`), 3 files added (new Identity migration trio). | The wire-contract preservation is the load-bearing call here: changing it would have flipped the user-profile form, the CP grids, every admin endpoint, the Website's `/account/profile`, the Flutter app's eventual binding, and every JSON-snapshot test simultaneously — all for an internal-id representation choice that callers don't need to know. ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 is the natural API surface; the numeric Id is the natural DB surface; translation at the boundary is one focused responsibility in one method per direction. Direct `SimfAppDbContext` injection over a new abstraction matches §11 §17 minimum-viable-diff — adding `ICountryLookup` would have introduced an interface + impl + DI registration + a sync-vs-async surface decision, all to wrap a two-line `.Where().Select().SingleOrDefaultAsync()` that already lives at the Infrastructure layer. The decision to regenerate a clean `InitialCreate` rather than carry an additive `ChangeNationalityCodeToNationalityId` migration matches D-151's stated consequence (the freeze is removed precisely so changes like this can be flat one-shot migrations rather than per-decision rationale exercises); the prior `InitialCreate` had only ever applied to dev DBs, never to production. Retiring `SIMF.Common.Countries` is the natural consequence of Phase A landing a Country CRUD: the static list and the DB table would have been two sources of truth competing for the same picker, and the test that asserts `Seed_includes_Saudi_Arabia_at_id_682` is now the single contract for "what countries exist". The catastrophic 281/294 failure during the first test run after the migration regen was a stale-test-bin issue resolved by a `--no-incremental` rebuild — a useful reminder that EF migration regen invalidates the test project's transitive references and requires a clean rebuild before `dotnet test --no-build`. |

From now until the pre-event reinstatement, the team may: (a) add, alter or drop any table on either DbContext via additive reversible migrations; (b) add new values to existing enums (rename/reorder still discouraged but no longer hard-blocked); (c) write new migrations against either context's history ledger as needed. **What remains guarded:** (i) public wire contract — `ApiResult<T>` envelope, `ErrorCodes` namespacing, existing DTO field names — still requires per-decision rationale for any breaking change; (ii) every new entity ships with its EF config + migration in the same commit; migrations must be reversible (`Down`) unless a per-decision rationale exempts them; (iii) every write still flows through the D-109 row-audit interceptor unless the entity is itself an audit log (e.g. `GateScan`). **Reinstatement plan:** the freeze is reinstated when the system enters pre-event smoke; a follow-up entry will record the reinstatement point and the schema baseline at that time. **Immediate consequences:** (a) `UserProfile.NationalityCode → NationalityId` is now a normal additive migration on `SimfIdentityDbContext`; (b) Country lookup CRUD lands on `SimfAppDbContext` (with a manually-assigned `Id int` per the team's standard list; SA = 113, code "SA", phone prefix "+966"; seeded inline); (c) Speaker enhancement (CountryId FK + UserProfileId + Arabic counterparts + consent toggles + social URLs) lands as one additive migration on top of the in-flight `AddSpeakers` basic migration; (d) D-148's `GateScan` row-audit opt-out and D-135's five carry-forward constraints still apply as engineering discipline even though their freeze-derived enforcement is gone. | The freeze had served its stability purpose during the Login-API release but was now generating friction without buying defensive value: the team is actively iterating on Programme + Identity schema changes and every change required a per-decision rationale that read like ceremony rather than judgement. The five carry-forward constraints preserve every D-110 benefit (reversible migrations, single ledger, additive enums, row-audited writes, Identity stability) while unblocking the schema additions the planned next sprints need. Keeping the Identity schema frozen means user authentication + role + permission machinery remains the locked baseline operators can rely on, while the business domain (Themes, Sessions, Bookings, etc.) is free to grow. The owner's one-word "Approve" carries the same authority as a full §11 pre-approval block per CLAUDE.md §1 since the proposal text was reviewed in `SIMF-D134-Module-Build-Plan.md` §6 before the approval landed. |

Surface: `/admin/attendees` (canonical D-117 + D-132 read-only grid + filter row): Kind dropdown (All / Visitors only / Others only) + State dropdown (Any / Approved / Pending / Rejected) + email-or-display-name search + Apply / Clear. Screenshot: `docs/screenshots/d134-attendees.png`. **Freeze (D-110):** zero EF schema / migration / enum impact. **Sprint A close-out:** Roles (D-134-A1) + Operation log (D-134-A2) + Attendees (this) = **3/22 D-134 stubs graduated to Real**, all Path 2 / no migration / no D-135 needed. Sprint A delivered ~8 new backend files, 6 new frontend files, ~250 resx keys total, 3 per-page docs, 3 E2E catalogue files, 3 Admin Manual chapters, 3 decisions-log entries. **The remaining 19 P1.lift modules are blocked on D-135 freeze-lift** before Sprints B–E can begin. **Deferred (explicit in page doc + manual):** XLSX export on Attendees + Operation log; permission grant editor + assign-to-user on Roles; date-range filter UI on Operation log. | Attendees exercises the **read-only-with-multi-filter** pattern — combined with Roles' canonical CRUD and Operation log's audit-table read, the three Sprint-A modules cover every Path 2 shape the plan promised. The filter row uses standard primitives + an Apply button rather than auto-filter-on-keystroke because EF-side LIKE searches over the audit table benefit from explicit user intent — auto-filtering every keystroke would hammer the DB with throwaway queries. The audit-log-is-not-itself-audited choice (no `Admin.AuditLogViewed` event) was a deliberate non-action — the D-109 interceptor fires on writes only, this is a read, and adding a write-on-read just to "audit the audit" would dilute the signal. The page doc explicitly documents this non-behaviour so a reviewer doesn't mistake it for an oversight. |

Choosing the migrated `/admin/interests` as the sample page is deliberate: it's the freshest in the team's context (D-132 just shipped this hour), it's the simplest CRUD shape, and it exercises every section of the template (banner, modals, soft-delete, bilingual fields, edge cases). The 13-section template is biased toward CRUD pages because that's most of the CP — non-CRUD pages (Print badge desk, Logs viewer, Reset 2FA, Auth pages) will populate fewer sections (mark unused ones "N/A — {reason}") rather than warp the template to fit them. The Admin Manual chapter for Interests deliberately includes screenshots, troubleshooting, and "what you cannot do" — that last subsection is the most operator-valued in the field, because operators waste hours trying to do things that aren't supported. The folder split (`docs/pages/` for reference / `docs/manuals/` for narrative) keeps the daily-operator audience separate from the per-page schema; the test catalogue lives a third place (`docs/tests/e2e/`) because tests cross-reference both. The owner will see the cost: each subsequent per-page doc is ~250 lines, each manual chapter ~150 lines, each E2E entry ~100 lines, each UCS entry ~50 lines — so ~550 lines per CRUD page × ~30 pages = ~16 000 lines of documentation across 5+ commits. That estimate is honest before they accept the spend. |

The DTO carries `ProfileTypeId` AND `ProfileTypeName` (+ Arabic) so the CP modal can render the human label without a second lookup. The left-join on `UserProfiles` is correct because admin-created Visitor / Other accounts can land in PendingApproval before the user fills the profile form — without it the read would fail for those rows, defeating its purpose. |

Without the first script, the bridge is incomplete — the component renders the dialog and the cropper canvas tries to come up, but on `CropperComponent.DisposeAsync()` the JS-interop call `cropper.destroy` resolves the path `window.cropper.destroy` and crashes with `Microsoft.JSInterop.JSException: Could not find 'cropper.destroy' ('cropper' was undefined).`, which tore down the SignalR circuit and forced a page reload. **End-to-end smoke proof:** with both scripts loaded, `window.cropper` is an object (verified via `evaluate_script`); injecting a PNG file via `DataTransfer + dispatchEvent('change')` opens the modal, now showing BOTH the "image to crop" canvas AND the "image to preview" pane (cropperjs is actually initialised now — before D-123 only the dialog frame appeared); clicking Cancel closes the modal cleanly with zero console errors and no `'cropper' undefined` crash. Screenshot reused — same modal shape as `docs/screenshots/d122-cropper-modal-opens.png`. **One-file diff:** `src/ControlPanel/SIMF.ControlPanel/Components/App.razor` — one new `<script>` line + a 4-line comment block. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release (CP only). **Tests:** unchanged 303 / 303. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No csproj edit, no DI edit, no Razor code edit** — only the script-load order in App.razor. | The cropper-on-dispose crash is the second half of "what V10 wires in App.razor that SIMF didn't". D-116 added `<link>` for `cropper.min.css` and `<script>` for `cropperJsInterop.min.js` but missed the first cropperjs script the interop bridge depends on. D-122 registered the cropper service in DI so the component would even render; this entry restores the cropperjs runtime so the rendered component doesn't crash when it's torn down. Both are pure "use as in erp exactly" fixes — the V10 file's two cropper script tags side-by-side were the canonical source of truth. The dispose path was missed in the D-122 smoke because the smoke didn't close the modal; this entry closes that hole. The 401-on-stale-session observation from D-122 is still out of scope and still owed a follow-up. |
| D-122 | 2026-05-28 | **Cropper.Blazor DI registration + SignalR message-size bump — finishes D-116 (cropper crashed on first render).** Adds the two pieces missing from D-116's CP `Program.cs`, mirrored byte-for-byte from V10 ERP's `Program.cs`: (a) `using Cropper.Blazor.Extensions;` + `builder.Services.AddCropper();` — without this DI registration, `CropperComponent` crashes at runtime the first time `<SimfImageCropperModal>` tries to render, with `System.InvalidOperationException: Cannot provide a value for property 'CropperJsIntertop' on type 'Cropper.Blazor.Components.CropperComponent'. There is no registered service of type 'Cropper.Blazor.Services.ICropperJsInterop'.` The package adds the type via an `IServiceCollection` extension method; the `AddCropper()` call is the only thing that registers the JS-interop service in DI. (b) `MaximumReceiveMessageSize` on `AddHubOptions` raised from 256 KB (originally sized for the QR SVG render diff) to **10 MB**, matching V10's `AddSignalR(... 10 * 1024 * 1024 ...)` (V10 Program.cs lines 43-46) — the cropper sends base64 data URLs of the source image (up to the 2 MB avatar policy) through JS interop over the same SignalR transport, so a >256 KB source PNG would fail mid-crop. **End-to-end smoke proof:** with the running CP rebuilt, a programmatic file injection into `<input id="avatar-input" type="file">` (via JS DataTransfer + `dispatchEvent('change')`) opened the `<dialog>Crop your avatar</dialog>` modal cleanly — Cancel + "Crop and save" buttons render, `CropperComponent` mounts without throwing, zero console errors. That's the canonical fix for "users get bounced to /login after `dotnet run` rotates the key", and is the natural companion to the 401 investigation. Out of scope for D-122. | The cropper was committed in D-116 with all the visual + component plumbing in place but missing the single line that registers `ICropperJsInterop` in DI — a runtime-only failure that the build, the test suite, and the bUnit-less smoke all let pass because none of them actually mount `<CropperComponent>`.
