| D-121 | 2026-05-27 | **Cookie-auth refresh hook — closes the stale-JWT 401 that blocked every JS-driven BFF call past the 30-minute mark.** Adds `SimfCookieRefreshHandler` (duplicated into both `SIMF.ControlPanel` and `SIMF.Web` — matching the established `SignInTicketStore` / `AuthEndpoints` split between the two host projects), wired into each project's cookie scheme via `options.Events.OnValidatePrincipal = SimfCookieRefreshHandler.OnValidatePrincipalAsync` in `Program.cs`. The hook reads `access_token` / `refresh_token` / `expires_at` from the cookie's stored tokens; when the access token has ≤2 minutes left (comfortably outside `JwtBearerSetup.cs`'s 30-second clock-skew tolerance), it calls `SimfAuthClient.RefreshAsync` and persists the rotated pair + a fresh `expires_at` back into the cookie, setting `ShouldRenew = true`. On a definitive refresh failure (refresh token revoked / expired / rejected) the handler `RejectPrincipal()`s + `SignOutAsync`'s — the user goes through `/login` again, the same end state the cookie's 8-hour expiry would have delivered, just reached sooner. A transient cancellation leaves the principal alone so a brief API blip does not log the user out. The sign-in endpoints in both projects now persist `expires_at` from `AuthTokens.AccessTokenExpiresInSeconds` alongside the existing access/refresh tokens (via the new `SimfCookieRefreshHandler.StoreTokens` helper) so the first cookie write is already correct — without that, the very next request would needlessly refresh. **Root cause (verified):** access-token lifetime = 30 minutes (`JwtOptions.AccessTokenMinutes = 30`); cookie lifetime = 8 hours with sliding renewal. Refresh-token rotation was already implemented end-to-end on the API (`POST /api/v1/auth/refresh` + `SessionService.RefreshAsync`) and exposed on `SimfAuthClient.RefreshAsync` — but **nothing in either web project ever called it**. So past 30 min, every `/account/api/*` BFF forwarder read an expired JWT from the cookie via `GetTokenAsync("access_token")` and forwarded it; the API's `JwtBearerSetup.OnTokenValidated` failed lifetime validation and returned the standard 401 envelope — even though the user's cookie was still valid for hours. The user saw "Authentication is required" on every JS-driven action with no clear remediation. **Why centralise in the cookie middleware (vs. per-endpoint retry):** the cookie-validate hook runs ahead of every authenticated request, so every existing BFF forwarder picks up the fresh token transparently — zero per-endpoint plumbing, zero changes to `AccountEndpoints.cs`. **Front-end consumer:** every `/account/api/*` route in both `SIMF.ControlPanel.Endpoints.AccountEndpoints` and `SIMF.Web.Endpoints.AccountEndpoints` immediately benefits — no per-route changes were needed. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** full suite still 303 / 303 (2 Application + 5 Domain + 13 ApiClient + 34 ControlPanel + 249 Api). **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact — API contract unchanged, only consumer wiring added. **No csproj edit:** the helper lives inside each host project (where the ASP.NET Core auth namespaces are already in scope via the Razor SDK), not in the shared `SIMF.ApiClient` class library — initial attempt at the latter failed compile with CS0234 (`Microsoft.AspNetCore` missing) and was reverted rather than dragging an ASP.NET dependency into a pure HttpClient lib (CLAUDE.md §1.7). **Future test:** `SimfCookieRefreshHandlerTests` placeholder noted in `// Tests:` headers — the handler runs in cookie middleware and is awkward to unit-test in isolation; a focused integration test (in-memory API + cookie scheme) is the right shape but is deferred to a separate task. **Hand-off delivered alongside this fix:** `docs/handoff/PROMPT-C-pending-profile-read.md` — standalone extract of plan §11C — Prompt C, ready to paste into a fresh backend session for the §11.3 scoped pending-profile read endpoints. | Stopped the "Authentication is required" wall on every JS-driven Control Panel action past 30 minutes since sign-in (smoke-test blocker for D-117 / D-118 / D-119 / D-120 verification). The same bug existed in the public Website auth path; fixed in the same change since the cause + fix shape are identical and `SignInTicketStore` already establishes the duplicated-by-design pattern between the two host projects. Centralising the rotation in the cookie scheme means every present and future BFF endpoint is correct by default, with no per-endpoint retry-on-401 plumbing to maintain. Failure mode of the rotation (revoked refresh token / API down) is identical to today's "cookie expired" UX — re-login at `/login` — so no new failure surface was introduced. |
| D-120 | 2026-05-26 | **ProfileTypes PageColor field gains a native colour-swatch picker (closes the D-118 polish note).** Replaces the plain `SimfTextField` for `PageColor` in `ProfileTypeForm.razor` with a paired text + `<input type="color">` swatch. Both bind to the same `_model.PageColor`; the text input stays the **source of truth** and accepts the existing free-text contract (CSS variables, 3-digit hex shortcuts, anything ≤ 32 chars). The swatch is a visual write-only shortcut that always writes `#rrggbb` back into the text field on change. When `_model.PageColor` is NOT a canonical `^#[0-9A-Fa-f]{6}$` hex (so the picker can't display it), the picker falls back to the brand navy `#244A77` for *display only* — no silent write happens until the user actually picks. New CSS in `simf-components.css`: `.simf-field__control--with-swatch` (flex row, gap, swatch hugs its content) + `.simf-field__color-swatch` (2.5-rem wide, token-driven border/radius/background, focus ring + disabled state — zero raw hex, zero inline styles). 1 new resx key per locale (en + ar): `Admin.ProfileTypes.Field.PageColorPicker` (aria-label for the swatch — required because the swatch has no visible text). No SimfTextField change — the new markup hand-rolls the `simf-field*` class structure that SimfTextField uses internally, keeping the shared primitive untouched per CLAUDE.md §1.6 (no scope creep). **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** full suite still 303 / 303. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No API change** — the text shape sent to `POST /admin/profile-types` / `PUT /admin/profile-types/{id}` is unchanged. | Closes the explicit follow-up note from D-118 ("PageColor was rendered as a plain text input… a real picker is a follow-up polish"). The picker-+-text pairing keeps the existing free-text capability that the D-115 contract allows (`PageColor` accepts `"#FFD700"`, `"var(--brand-blue)"`, etc.) — a picker-only field would have silently stripped CSS variables and 3-digit hex shortcuts on every save, which would surprise an admin who picked from a brand stylesheet. The text-as-source-of-truth direction was chosen because `<input type="color">` only renders `#rrggbb` values; making it the source of truth would force every model value into that narrow space. Falling back to `#244A77` for the swatch when the text isn't a 6-hex value gives the field a sensible appearance without surprising the user — the picker doesn't appear "broken" or "wrong" because the matching text is visible next to it. The new CSS classes are scoped to `.simf-field__control--with-swatch` so other surfaces can opt in by adding the modifier; the swatch class itself doesn't change the layout unless paired with the modifier. |

On cancel the pick is discarded and the file input is cleared so the same file fires the change event again. **New JS helpers** in `wwwroot/js/simf-account.js`: `readFileAsDataUrl(inputId)` (FileReader → `{ ok, dataUrl, fileName, mimeType }`), `uploadDataUrl(url, dataUrl, fileName)` (fetch → blob → multipart upload via existing endpoint), `clearFileInput(inputId)` (resets `input.value` without `.click()`, since `triggerFileInput` would re-open the OS picker). **`Profile.razor` markup change:** the explicit "Upload new image" `<SimfButton>` is removed — picking a file now opens the cropper directly, and the cropper's own "Crop and save" button replaces the previous Upload action. The "Remove avatar" secondary button stays. The hidden `<input type="file">` gains an `@onchange="OnAvatarFilePickedAsync"` handler. **3 new resx keys per locale** (en + ar): `Profile.Avatar.Crop.Title`, `Profile.Avatar.PickFailed`, `Profile.Avatar.UploadFailed`. The legacy `Profile.Avatar.Upload` key stays orphaned per CLAUDE.md §1.6 (no hidden cleanup). **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** 303 / 303 still passing. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No API change** — the cropper outputs a data URL, the JS helper decodes it to bytes, and the existing multipart endpoint accepts it transparently. | Closes UI plan §11.5's "first consumer" follow-up. The avatar flow is the cleanest first consumer because it's already a single-file upload with the right shape (square output ≈ avatar) and the existing endpoint accepts arbitrary image bytes. Removing the explicit Upload button is a UX simplification — picking immediately means "I want to use this image", so an extra confirm step before the cropper would be friction; the cropper's own Save action is the confirm. Going through the existing multipart endpoint (rather than adding a new base64-accepting endpoint) keeps the backend untouched per §1.7 and avoids two parallel write paths for the same file. The data-URL → blob conversion happens in the browser via `fetch(dataUrl)` which is the modern idiomatic shortcut over manually base64-decoding into a typed array. The `clearFileInput` helper is a thin sibling of `triggerFileInput` rather than a `triggerFileInput(silent: true)` variant because the call sites read more clearly with two named methods than one boolean flag. |

Service surface: `IAdminUserBulkService` gains four additive `*ByKindAsync` methods (`BulkDeleteUsersByKindAsync`, `DuplicateUserByKindAsync`, `ExportUsersByKindAsync`, `ImportUsersByKindAsync`) implemented on the same `AdminAccountService` (keeps the R2 factory-delegate registration intact). **Type-smuggling guards:** bulk-delete silently skips any subject whose `UserType` doesn't match the kind (audited as `AdminUserNotFound`); duplicate returns 404 for wrong-type source (same code as a missing source — never reveals "id exists but is the wrong type"); export filters both selected-ids and whole-set paths by `UserType` before projection; import forces every created user to the route kind regardless of any `Role` column in the spreadsheet (the Admin-role smuggle path is closed). **Excel additive change:** `UserImportRow` gains an optional `Guid? ProfileTypeId`; `ClosedXmlUserExcelService.Parse` reads it from an optional `ProfileTypeId` header column when present (the existing Admin import never emits one and stays bit-for-bit). The Other import requires a parseable `ProfileTypeId` per row (matches the single-create-Other validator); rows missing it land in the error report. **Tests:** new `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/AdminGridVisitorsTests.cs` (5) + `AdminGridOthersTests.cs` (5) covering bulk-delete cross-type skip, duplicate-of-wrong-type → 404, export cross-type filter, import type-smuggling guard, non-admin → 403, and the Other-kind mandatory ProfileTypeId path. Build: 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. Full suite: 303/303. **Frontend consumer:** `src/ControlPanel/SIMF.ControlPanel/Components/Pages/Admin/VisitorsList.razor` + `OthersList.razor` (to be wired in the D-114-tracked CP increment). **Freeze (D-110):** zero EF schema / migration / enum changes; `UserImportRow` extension is additive (record nullable parameter with default `null`). | Mirrors the existing `/admin/admins/*` shape — same `ApiResult<T>` envelope, same `Policies(...)`, same XLSX MIME, same 5 MB cap + ZIP-magic check on import. The wrong-type response choice was the load-bearing security call: 404 not 403, and identical to the unknown-id branch, so a probing admin can't enumerate cross-type ids by error-code diff. The optional `ProfileTypeId` column on `UserImportRow` was the minimum-viable diff to support Other-kind import without touching the Admin-kind round-trip; alternatives (per-kind row types, two parser implementations) would have multiplied surface for no behaviour win. New methods are on the same `AdminAccountService` because the R2 follow-up (D-100) only carved off the query service; the bulk methods share private helpers (`GetAdministratorRoleIdAsync`, the transactional runner) with the existing `BulkDeleteUsersAsync` — a separate `AdminUserBulkByKindService` would have either duplicated those helpers or chained back through DI for no gain. The Admin-vs-Administrator + self-delete guard is preserved in the by-kind path: an Administrator-roled Visitor / Other in the batch is still skipped silently, so a stray privileged subject can't be deleted via the visitor / other surface. Validators were not duplicated because the DTO shape is identical; one validator class covers all six endpoint instances. |

Injects only `SimfIdentityDbContext` — the method is a pure read, no shared private helpers with the rest of `AdminAccountService`. `AdminAccountService` drops `IAdminProfileTypeQueryService` from its base list + drops the method body (replaced by a one-line comment pointing at the new class). DI registration in `DependencyInjection.cs` changes from `services.AddScoped<IAdminProfileTypeQueryService>(sp => sp.GetRequiredService<AdminAccountService>())` (the R2 factory-delegate pattern) to a direct `services.AddScoped<IAdminProfileTypeQueryService, AdminProfileTypeQueryService>()`. Each future slice follows the same shape: new class file, scoped DI registration replaces the factory delegate, method body deleted from `AdminAccountService`. The remaining four interfaces are deferred because the impl reuses rich private helpers — extracting them safely requires either (a) duplicating those helpers per slice (anti-DRY), (b) hoisting them into a new shared `AdminAccountSupport` collaborator (worth doing, but a separate refactor that needs its own test pass), or (c) accepting a temporary period where slices are partial — none of those are appropriate to ram through in a marathon session. The slice that landed here is the "free" one; the others should be picked up in a dedicated R2-finish session. |
| D-099 | 2026-05-26 | **Credential-flow emails also dispatch an in-app notification.** Closes backlog item §3 L33. New `NotificationDispatcherExtensions.TryDispatchAsync(dispatcher, request, logger, ct)` mirrors the H23 / D-083 `IEmailQueue.TryEnqueueAsync` shape — wraps `INotificationDispatcher.DispatchAsync` with a log-and-swallow guard so an in-app dispatch failure does not break the credential flow that has already returned to the caller. `PasswordService.ForgotPasswordAsync`, `RegistrationService.SignUpAsync`, `RegistrationService.ResendCodeAsync`, and `SignInService.SignInAsync` (email-OTP branch only — TOTP returns earlier with no email) each get a `notifications.TryDispatchAsync(...)` call right after their existing `emailQueue.TryEnqueueAsync(...)` site. Each dispatch carries `SendEmail = false` (the email was already queued; this is the in-app trail only) and bilingual `Title` / `TitleArabic` / `Body` / `BodyArabic`. Four new Kinds: `Credential.PasswordResetRequested`, `Credential.EmailVerificationSent`, `Credential.EmailVerificationResent`, `Credential.SignInOtpSent`. `NotificationTests.CreateUserAndSignInAsync` test helper now calls a new `ClearNotificationsAsync(userId)` step (`ExecuteDeleteAsync` against the `Notifications` table) so the three existing count-based assertions (List, UnreadCount, Dispatcher) start from a clean slate — without the clear, sign-up's new in-app notification would be the 1st row each test sees. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes the last small follow-up from §3 of the backlog. The notification gives the user a visible "yes, this email was sent" trail inside the app after sign-in (pre-auth flows like forgot-password and sign-up create the notification before the user has a session; they see it later). The TryDispatch extension matches the H23 TryEnqueue shape so the credential services have one consistent pattern for "fire-and-forget side-effect after the persisted commit". Notification kinds use a stable `Credential.*` prefix so a future filter (e.g. "hide credential trail by default") can target them. `SendEmail = false` is the load-bearing flag — without it the dispatcher would queue a duplicate email. Test-helper clear pattern is cleaner than per-test +1 counting because the +1 might shift again as new credential kinds are added; tests stay readable. |

| D-097 | 2026-05-26 | **IT / Support email distribution list for send failures.** New `EmailOptions.FailureAlertRecipients` field — a comma-, semicolon- or whitespace-separated string of operations addresses. When configured, `EmailBackgroundService` sends a short alert email to each address whenever an outbound message fails to send (SMTP rejected, network outage, etc.). The recipient list is parsed once per failure (`Split(RecipientDelimiters, RemoveEmptyEntries \| TrimEntries)` + distinct, case-insensitive). The alert email's body carries the failed recipient, the original subject, and the exception type + message (HTML-encoded). The alert path is fire-and-forget — if the alert ITSELF fails to send, the exception is logged but no second alert is fired to avoid amplifying a sustained SMTP outage into a per-recipient storm. Default value is empty, so existing deployments without a configured DL stay quiet (the audit row from `Email.EnqueueFailed`, H10/D-065, is still written either way). Tests: 222 / 222 unchanged (the failure-alert dispatch is a side-effect on a different background worker; the existing `EmailEnqueueFailureTests` continue to exercise the audit-row path). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes backlog item §3 L32 ("Email distribution list — Support / IT recipients for failure-alert emails"). The integration point is the background sender (not `TryEnqueueAsync` in Application) because (a) the most common failure mode is SMTP send failure, not enqueue failure — the queue is in-memory and rarely fails; (b) putting the alert path in the background service keeps Application code free of an operational concern; (c) the existing `OperationLog` audit row covers the enqueue-failure case, and the alert addresses are written into `EmailOptions` which is an Infrastructure concern. No-recurse-on-alert-failure is the load-bearing safety: a sustained SMTP outage would otherwise issue one alert per failed message per configured recipient, multiplying the storm. The empty default preserves the no-op posture for any deployment that hasn't opted in yet — the alert is purely additive. |

New `GET /api/v1/auth/totp/pairing` endpoint (`TotpPairingEndpoint.cs`) is bearer-protected and rate-limited under the `"auth"` policy; returns 404 when the account has no active secret. New `SimfAccountClient.TotpPairingAsync` API-client method. CP proxy: new `MapGet("/totp/pairing", ...)` in `AccountEndpoints.cs`. New `/account/totp-pairing` Blazor page (`Components/Pages/Account/TotpPairing.razor`) loads via JS interop, renders the QR SVG + secret text, and routes the user to the Profile page when no active secret exists. New EN + AR resx keys under the `TotpPairing.*` namespace (Title, Instruction, SecretHint, NoSecret, GoToProfile, Loading, LoadError). Two new integration tests: `Pairing_returns_404_when_the_account_has_no_active_secret`, `Pairing_returns_the_same_QR_for_the_enrolled_users_active_secret` (proves idempotency vs the rotating Setup endpoint, with the post-Confirm refresh-token rotation noted because the security stamp rolls on enrolment). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Owner reported the seeded super-admin's TOTP secret (`dbji csx7 c3mj s2qa sjcl rbcl kiqk ovr3`) was "not working" — most likely cause is a paired-then-unpaired authenticator that needs to re-scan the SAME secret, not a rotation. The standard `POST /auth/totp/setup` rotates the secret on every call, which would break sign-in for an admin who had already paired once. The new pairing endpoint is the read-only counterpart: same secret, same QR, every call. The endpoint is bearer-protected (the admin has to sign in first — which they can do via the bypass `super-admin sign-in is allowed without TOTP for the seeded admin` only if such bypass exists; for the typical case the admin still needs TOTP, which is the chicken-and-egg this fix solves PROVIDED the seeded secret matches what they have in their authenticator). If the authenticator is truly lost (no recovery codes, no other paired device, the seeded secret was rotated), the admin needs the next-tier fix: a SuperAdmin-only "reset my own TOTP" path. That's a follow-up. The test that proves the same QR is returned across calls is the load-bearing contract — without it a future change to the Setup endpoint that accidentally rotates the active secret would silently break the pairing page. |

| D-094 | 2026-05-26 | **R3.5 — Split `IUserAccountRepository` into 5 role-cohesive sub-interfaces.** Five new interfaces in `src/Backend/SIMF.Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/`: `IUserAccountStore` (5 methods — Find by email/id, Create ×2, Update), `IUserCredentialStore` (5 — Check/Add/Remove/ChangePassword + UpdateSecurityStamp), `IUserLockoutTracker` (3 — IsLockedOut, AccessFailed, ResetAccessFailedCount), `IUserRoleStore` (4 — GetRoles, IsInRole, AddToRole, RemoveFromRoles), `IUserTwoFactorStore` (5 — GetAuthenticatorKey, Set/Get/RemoveAuthToken, SetTwoFactorEnabled). `IUserAccountRepository` is now a marker that inherits all five — existing consumers (six Application services + IdentitySeeder) compile unchanged. DI updates: one scoped `UserAccountRepository` instance backs all six registrations (aggregate + five sub-interfaces) via factory delegates so the change-tracker scope and per-request state stay shared (same pattern R2 — D-075 used for `AdminAccountService`). New code is encouraged to inject only the narrower contract it actually uses, so constructor signatures advertise the concerns the service touches. Tests: **275 / 275** unchanged. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes post-R3 review SEV-1.2 (Architecture lens). The 22-method aggregate was a method-for-method port of `UserManager<TUser>` — fine as the transitional surface during R3, but it forced every Application service to depend on every concern. With the seam split, the next time someone reads `SignInService`'s constructor they can see immediately that it touches users, credentials, lockout and roles, but not 2FA tokens — exactly the kind of constructor-as-documentation `§17` ("design for change") recommends. The marker-interface inheritance keeps the migration cost zero for existing services; new services can pick the narrow seam by default. Sub-interface migration of the existing services is a low-priority follow-up — no functional benefit, only signature clarity. The DI shape (one scoped impl, six registrations pointing at it) is structurally identical to R2's `AdminAccountService` split (D-075), so the pattern is consistent across both interface decompositions. |

| D-090 | 2026-05-26 | **R5a — Pure-POCO Domain, slice 1: introduce `IdentitySimfUser` persistence shim; re-point EF + Identity wiring; bidirectional proto-mapper in `UserAccountRepository`.** First slice of the R5 sprint per `docs/SIMF-Architecture-Refactor-Plan.md`. New `src/Backend/SIMF.Infrastructure/Identity/IdentitySimfUser.cs` mirrors the Domain `SimfUser` field-for-field and is the type EF tracks for the `AspNetUsers` table. `SimfIdentityDbContext` re-types its base from `IdentityDbContext<SimfUser, SimfRole, Guid>` to `IdentityDbContext<IdentitySimfUser, SimfRole, Guid>`; `SimfUserConfiguration` re-binds to `IEntityTypeConfiguration<IdentitySimfUser>`; `DependencyInjection.AddIdentityCore` re-types to `IdentitySimfUser`. Four FK-bearing configurations (`AccountCode`, `RefreshToken`, `SecondFactorToken`, `TotpRecoveryCode`) swap their `HasOne<SimfUser>()` to `HasOne<IdentitySimfUser>()` so EF stops discovering the Domain `SimfUser` as a duplicate entity that would demand its own table. `UserAccountRepository` rewritten around the merge-into-tracked pattern: a new `EnsureTrackedAsync(SimfUser)` reuses the already-tracked `IdentitySimfUser` from a prior `FindBy*` (the common case) or fetches a fresh tracked instance via `UserManager.FindByIdAsync`; `ApplyDomainMutations` copies caller mutations onto the tracked entity; `SyncBack` copies server-generated fields back. This preserves EF's concurrency-stamp snapshot — without it, every `find-then-update` cycle hits `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` (the snapshot's "original" ConcurrencyStamp equals the just-set new GUID, so the `WHERE ConcurrencyStamp = @old` matches zero rows). A snapshot-only EF migration (`R5aRebindUserEntityToIdentitySimfUser`) with empty `Up`/`Down` regenerates the model snapshot to point at `IdentitySimfUser` — DB schema unchanged. `IdentitySimfUser` is `public sealed` rather than `internal sealed` because C# requires a generic type parameter on a public class (`SimfIdentityDbContext`) to be at least as accessible. Test-fixture sweep: the **12 test files** that injected `UserManager<SimfUser>` directly from DI and the corresponding `new SimfUser { ... }` constructor sites swap to `UserManager<IdentitySimfUser>` and `new IdentitySimfUser { ... }` — purely mechanical, no semantic change. Tests: **220 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 272 / 272**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. R5b (extract `IdentityUserMapper` from the proto-mapper), R5c (re-shape repository return types around it), R5d–e (migrate Application and API consumers), R5f (drop `IdentityUser<Guid>` from `SimfUser`'s inheritance), R5g (drop `Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Stores` from `SIMF.Domain.csproj`) remain queued. | First step of R5 per the architecture refactor plan. The seam is the type EF tracks — every other slice of R5 piggy-backs on this one. The Domain `SimfUser` still inherits `IdentityUser<Guid>` (R5f does the actual decoupling); R5a just gives Identity a Domain-free type to project rows into. The merge-into-tracked design replaces an earlier detach-after attempt that hit the "another instance with the same key is already tracked" guard (UserManager's internal `Context.Attach` does not tolerate a duplicate, and detaching the FindBy-tracked entity from the change tracker wasn't always sufficient in scope). Re-tracking-then-merging means UserManager always operates on the entity it would have loaded for itself — concurrency stamps line up, second-find queries find the existing tracked entry (no extra SELECT), and EF's identity map stays clean. The extra `FindByIdAsync` round-trip on writes that come WITHOUT a prior `FindByEmail/Id` is the cost of carving the seam in a single tractable commit; R5c removes it by re-shaping the repository contract to return the tracked entity directly. The proto-mapper's bidirectional copy is the §17 minimum that lets every caller — Application services and test fixtures — keep speaking `SimfUser`. The FK configuration fix is load-bearing: without it, the empty migration EF wanted to generate had `CreateTable("SimfUser")` plus 4 `DropForeignKey`/`AddForeignKey` pairs, because EF's convention scanner discovered Domain `SimfUser` through the orphan `HasOne<SimfUser>()` lines and treated it as a separate entity that needed its own table. Pointing the FKs at `IdentitySimfUser` is the canonical fix and produces the truly empty migration. The 12-test-file fixture sweep was the bulk of the test work — the repository contract is unchanged, so application-flow tests pass without modification; only fixtures that bypass the repo to seed users directly via `UserManager` needed the type swap. |
| D-088 | 2026-05-26 | **H28 + H29 — Cancellation-token entry checks on `UserAccountRepository`; default per-IP global rate-limit on every request.** Two findings from the post-R3 review closed in one commit. **(H28 / Finding I)** Every method on `UserAccountRepository` (the `IUserAccountRepository` implementation in Infrastructure) calls `cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` at entry. A pre-cancelled call now throws immediately instead of running the `UserManager` operation to completion — making the `CancellationToken` parameter on the interface honest at the boundary. `UserManager`'s public API does not accept tokens, so true mid-operation cancellation is the R5-level swap to a Domain-owned user store (queued in the follow-up backlog); the entry-throw is the §17 minimum that satisfies the contract today. **(H29 / Security SEV-2.1 main)** `Program.cs`'s `AddRateLimiter` registers a `GlobalLimiter` via `PartitionedRateLimiter.Create<HttpContext, string>` keyed on `Connection.RemoteIpAddress?.ToString() ?? "unknown"` with a per-IP fixed-window cap of `GlobalPermitLimit / GlobalWindowSeconds` (defaults 600 / 60s). The global cap applies to EVERY request, stacking with the per-route `"auth"` and `"auth-email"` policies for credential flows — pre-H29 bearer-protected routes had no per-IP cap, so a malformed-bearer flood could pin a CPU on token validation. `RateLimitOptions` gains `GlobalPermitLimit` (600) and `GlobalWindowSeconds` (60); `SimfApiFactory` test default sets `RateLimit__GlobalPermitLimit=1000000` so long-running test classes that hit hundreds of endpoints don't trip the cap. Tests: **220 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 272 / 272** unchanged. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | (H28) Closes the half of post-R3 Finding I that is satisfiable without R5. The reviewer's deeper point — "the impl drops the token because UserManager doesn't accept one" — is structurally true and stays true until the user-store swap. Calling `ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` at entry costs one comparison per method but eliminates the "I passed a cancelled token and the operation still ran" failure mode at the cheapest possible cost. (H29) Closes the main half of post-R3 Security SEV-2.1. The pre-H29 rate limiter only covered `/auth/*`; bearer-protected routes (the entire `/admin/*` surface, `/account/*`, `/api/v1/notifications/*`, etc.) had no per-IP rate cap. A flood from one IP on any of those routes — malformed bearer or otherwise — could starve the server. The global limiter is permissive enough that real traffic never sees it (600/min/IP = 10/s; legitimate clients are well below that even during bursty UI navigation) and tight enough that abuse traffic hits the cap fast. The cap stacks with the per-route policies — `/auth/sign-in` is now bounded by THREE concurrent partitions (global per-IP + auth per-IP + auth-email per-email); the strictest one wins per request. The remaining Security SEV-2.1 sub-finding — fire-and-forget audit channel — is queued as a Bucket 3 follow-up; H26 (D-086) already caps the worst single offender (bearer-rejection audit writes). |

| D-086 | 2026-05-26 | **H26 — Per-IP audit-DB write throttle on bearer rejections (Security SEV-2.1 part 2).** `JwtBearerSetup.AuditRejectionAsync` now counts bearer rejections per source IP in an `IMemoryCache`-backed 60-second sliding window keyed `simf-bearer-rej:{ip}`. The 11th rejection from the same IP in that window skips the synchronous `IAuditLog.WriteAsync` (drops the DB row) but the structured `ILogger<JwtBearerOptions>.LogWarning("Bearer rejection from {Ip}: {Detail}")` line ALWAYS fires — SOC's log forwarder still sees the storm. After the cap is reached, every further hit also writes a "Bearer rejection audit suppressed" warning so the storm duration is visible. Cap constants: `MaxAuditWritesPerWindow = 10`, `AuditWindow = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(1)`. New DI registration: `services.AddMemoryCache()` in `Program.cs`. Regression test in `JwtMiddlewareTests.Bearer_rejection_audit_writes_are_capped_per_ip_within_the_window`: clears the rejection counter, fires 15 bearer-garbage requests, asserts the audit-row delta is `<= 10` and `>= 1`. Tests: **220 API** (219 + 1 H26) + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **272 / 272**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Security SEV-2.1 from the post-R3 5-agent review. Pre-H26 every malformed-bearer request synchronously wrote a DB row through `AuditLog.WriteAsync`'s `SaveChangesAsync` — an attacker firing `Authorization: Bearer <garbage>` against any protected endpoint forced one DB INSERT per request, with no rate limit on that route (the rate limiter's `"auth"` partition is bound only to the `/auth/*` endpoints; bearer-protected endpoints are not covered). The throttle is the minimum-viable DoS guard the post-R3 reviewer specifically named. A fuller fix — a fire-and-forget audit channel mirroring `EmailQueue` + `EmailBackgroundService`, batching inserts asynchronously — is queued for a later operations sprint (the audit log is not the only synchronous side-effect on the request path; `IRequestContext` writes are too). The per-IP cap keyed on `Connection.RemoteIpAddress?.ToString() ?? "unknown"` — same shape as the per-IP rate-limit partition in H7 (D-062), so a misrouted no-IP batch all shares one `"unknown"` bucket. Defence-in-depth: even if the cache misses (it's registered via `AddMemoryCache` which uses an in-process implementation; multi-instance deployments would need a distributed cache to share state), the request still pays the per-route auth cost; only the audit DB write is gated. |
| D-085 | 2026-05-26 | **H25 — End-to-end visitor-lifecycle test.** New `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/VisitorLifecycleTests.cs` drives the full chain in one `[Fact]`: sign-up → Registered → verify-email → EmailVerified → Web-audience sign-in (gets pre-profile tokens) → profile upsert (with one seeded `Interest`) triggers H2 (D-057) auto-transition to PendingApproval → admin (created in-test, audience=Cp) approves → Approved + `QrId` minted → final App-audience sign-in returns a JWT carrying `account_state=Approved` + `user_type=Visitor` (asserted via direct JWT claim read). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Sprint 1 §3.5 (DEFER) — the one Reality reviewer Finding 9 that was definite-do-eventually. Pre-H25, each lifecycle hop had its own test (sign-up, verify-email, profile upsert, admin approve, App-audience sign-in) but no test exercised them in chain — a regression at any boundary between two hops (e.g. an Approved visitor's JWT carries the wrong `user_type`, or H2's transaction commits but the security stamp doesn't roll) would have been invisible. The single-Fact shape is deliberate: failures land on the line of the failed step rather than scattered across the suite, the test's diff IS the lifecycle contract, and the test reads top-to-bottom like the user's actual journey. | D-082 | 2026-05-25 | **H20 + H21 — SIMF-owned `UserOperationResult` replaces `IdentityResult` on `IUserAccountRepository`; discarded results now throw via `.EnsureSuccessAsync()`.** Two findings from the post-R3 review closed in one commit. **(H21 / Finding C)** New `UserOperationResult` record + `UserOperationError` record in `src/Backend/SIMF.Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/UserOperationResult.cs` — SIMF-owned, no `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` reference. The `Code` strings stay Identity-compatible (e.g. `"PasswordMismatch"`, `"DuplicateEmail"`) so the existing `IdentityErrorTranslator` switch arms match the new shape unchanged. `IUserAccountRepository` updated: 12 method signatures change return type from `Task<IdentityResult>` to `Task<UserOperationResult>`. The `using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` line is removed from the interface file — Application no longer transitively depends on Identity types (closes the residual Architecture SEV-1.1 the post-R3 review flagged). `UserAccountRepository` implementation adds a private `Translate(IdentityResult) → UserOperationResult` helper called at the wrapper boundary; the framework primitive stays in Infrastructure. `IdentityErrorTranslator.ToArabic` parameter changes from `IdentityError` to `UserOperationError`. `PasswordService.PasswordRejected` helper signature follows the same swap. **(H20 / Finding D)** New `UserOperationResultExtensions.EnsureSuccessAsync(Task<UserOperationResult>)` extension throws `InvalidOperationException` with the joined error codes when the operation failed. Applied to **15 previously-discarded call sites** across `SignInService` (1), `PasswordService` (1), `RegistrationService` (1), `AdminAccountService` (8 — including the 2FA reset wipe sequence and 3 visitor-state-change updates), `IdentitySeeder` (4), `TotpEnrollmentService` (6 — every enrol / confirm / disable write), `AccountService` (2). Sites that explicitly check `result.Succeeded` are unchanged — they keep their existing flow. The chosen sites are the ones where a silent failure would corrupt security-relevant state: e.g. `SignInService.VerifyTotpAsync` writes `LastUsedTotpTimestep` (silent failure defeats the TOTP replay guard); `AdminAccountService.ResetTwoFactorAsync` wipes 2FA tokens (silent failure makes the audit log lie). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Architecture SEV-1.1 (post-R3) and Security SEV-1.3 + Code SEV-1.1 from the post-R3 5-agent review in one commit. The post-R3 review correctly observed that R3 "hid the verb (`UserManager`), not the nouns" — `IdentityResult` and `IdentityError` were still on the Application-facing contract, and Application's `.csproj` would not compile if Domain stopped re-exporting Identity. The fix is the SIMF-owned record with a 1:1 shape, so the translation at the wrapper boundary is straightforward and the Identity Code strings (which downstream consumers like `IdentityErrorTranslator` switch on) survive the swap. The reviewer also noted that 18 of 19 IdentityResult-returning call sites discarded the result — `EnsureSuccessAsync` is the §17 minimum-invasive fix: a single `.EnsureSuccessAsync()` per site is mechanical, gives every previously-silent failure a stack trace, and doesn't disturb the explicit-check sites. Three sites that aren't `UpdateAsync`/`Add*`/`Set*` patterns and one site where the existing flow already checks `.Succeeded` are intentionally unchanged. The exception type chosen is `InvalidOperationException` (not a domain exception) because (a) the error middleware maps it to 500 — appropriate for a failed write that the caller had no chance to validate against, and (b) introducing a SIMF-domain exception type for "user store said no" would invite explicit `try/catch` blocks at every call site, recreating the boilerplate the extension is supposed to remove. |

| D-080 | 2026-05-25 | **H19 — PasswordChangeRequired enforced at every token-mint path; body-buffer cap on the per-email rate-limit middleware; CheckAccountState now fires before the password-change gate.** Combined commit closes Security SEV-1.1 + SEV-1.2 + Code SEV-2.3 from the post-R3 5-agent review. Three changes: **(A)** New helper `RequirePasswordChangeNotRequired(SimfUser)` in `SignInService` throws `ApiException(AuthPasswordChangeRequired, 403)` whenever the flag is set. Called inside `VerifyTotpAsync`, `VerifyRecoveryCodeAsync`, `VerifyOtpAsync` (after `EnsureNotLockedOutAsync`), and `SessionService.RefreshAsync` (after the user is loaded by refresh-token-presented userId). Pre-H19 the H4 (D-059) gate only fired at the initial password step — a holder of a live MFA ticket or 30-day refresh token rotated forever after an admin set the flag. The full enforcement is now end-to-end across every mint surface. **(B)** `EmailRateLimitKeyMiddleware.TryReadEmailAsync` caps the body buffer at 8 KB: pre-buffer `Content-Length` guard refuses oversize bodies before any allocation; `EnableBuffering(bufferThreshold: 8192, bufferLimit: 8192)` caps the spill-to-disk too. Bare `catch` narrowed: `OperationCanceledException` propagates (was masked), `JsonException` returns null with a comment (legit parse failure → fall back to per-IP), `IOException` returns null (bufferLimit-exceeded mid-copy → same fallback). Closes Security SEV-1.2 + Security SEV-2.2 at the same time. **(C)** The `CheckAccountState` block now runs BEFORE the `PasswordChangeRequired` gate in `SignInService.SignInAsync` (was reversed pre-H19) — a Disabled / Registered user with the flag set previously got `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` (an enumeration oracle that told the attacker the account exists, is provisioned, and is just awaiting a reset). Now the disabled/unverified state-block fires first; the password-change gate only applies to live, signable accounts. Regression test in `SignInTests.Refresh_is_blocked_when_PasswordChangeRequired_is_set_after_sign_in`: signs in as a 2FA-disabled visitor, mints a refresh token, flips the flag, asserts `/auth/refresh` returns 403 + AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED. Tests: **218 API** (217 + 1 H19) + 32 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **268 / 268**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes three findings from the post-R3 review in one commit. (A) is the highest-impact security defect surfaced by the review: H4 (D-059) shipped as a single-chokepoint check at the password step; the review correctly pointed out that any holder of a downstream artefact (MFA ticket, refresh token) walked around it. The fix is the helper-method pattern so each mint surface gets the same gate — the helper is `private static` so future Verify methods can't forget to call it (a follow-up Roslyn analyzer could enforce this, but the call-site explicit pattern is fine for now). (B) closes the body-buffer DoS amplifier the review flagged: `EnableBuffering()` with no `bufferLimit` lets an attacker post a 30 MB JSON body and force a 30 MB MemoryStream allocation per request on the pre-auth path. 8 KB is well above legitimate credential bodies (< 1 KB in normal use) and far below the abuse threshold. Same commit narrows the catch to avoid masking cancellation, which the review's Finding J flagged separately. (C) is the smaller enumeration-oracle leak: the H4 comment block defended the original order ("the right password is the wrong door"), but the right door for a Disabled account is the disabled-account block; routing the flagged-disabled user via PasswordChangeRequired tells the attacker the account exists, is alive, and has just been credential-rotated — exactly the signal account-enumeration controls are supposed to suppress. The findings the review tagged for the bonus shape (rotating the security stamp + revoking refresh tokens when the flag is set) are not added in this commit because `AdminAccountService.ResetTwoFactorAsync` and other admin flows already do both unconditionally; the seeder path doesn't need them (no live tokens exist at first boot). |

The minter therefore moves to `SimfIdentityDbContext.Users` directly (the Infrastructure-internal seam where LINQ-shaped reads belong). | D-070 | 2026-05-25 | **H15 — `Pending_admin_is_blocked_on_every_admin_endpoint_class` theory parameterises the H1 gate across 14 admin routes.** One `[InlineData]` per endpoint class under `src/Backend/SIMF.Api/Endpoints/Admin/`: ListUsers, ListPending, CreateUser, BulkDeleteUsers, DuplicateUser, ExportUsers, ResetTwoFactor, ApproveStaff, RejectStaff, ApproveVisitor, RejectVisitor, Interest (one of five — same class), ListProfileTypes, Logs. | D-069 | 2026-05-25 | **H14 — `SimfFileUpload` exposes the most-recently-picked file in an `aria-live` region and composes the input's `aria-describedby` to include it.** Two new parameters: `PickedFilename` (the parent supplies after reading the file via JS interop, mirroring D-039's avatar-upload pattern) and `PickedLabel` (localised prefix like "Picked:" / "تم الاختيار:"). A `<p class="simf-fileupload__status" id="@_statusId" aria-live="polite">` is rendered immediately after the input; visible only when a filename is set, but kept in the DOM so live-region updates fire when the parent changes the parameter. `DescribedBy` now composes a space-separated list of relevant ids — error (if any) OR helper (otherwise), plus the status region when populated. New CSS: `.simf-fileupload__status` with `min-block-size: 1.25em` so the field shell doesn't visually jump when a filename lands, `visibility: hidden` on `:empty` so the reserved space doesn't read as awkward whitespace. Existing callers (visitor-profile ID-card upload) keep working without supplying the new parameters; `PickedFilename` defaults `null`, the region stays empty. Tests: **202 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 238 / 238** (markup-only; bUnit coverage for the affordance is queued in H17 alongside the bell tests). | Closes A11y SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. Before this commit the `SimfFileUpload` had a `<label for>` (good — the input's accessible name was correct) and an `aria-describedby` that pointed at either the helper or the error, but no surface for "you picked X". A keyboard-only user (or a screen-reader user) had no way to know the picker accepted their file before pressing the external Upload button — they had to either trust silence or look at the native input's "1 file selected" toggle, which assistive tech does not consistently announce. The fix is the standard `aria-live="polite"` status pattern: visible state for sighted keyboard users, announced state for screen readers. Keeping the region in the DOM (vs `display: none` when empty) is the live-region contract — the browser must hold a stable node for AT to observe its text changing. The bigger reviewer item — placing the Upload button inside the field shell with `aria-controls` to pair the two — is intentionally deferred: that's an API-shape change for every caller, and the live-region status closes the actual barrier (the user not knowing whether their pick registered) without breaking the component contract. |
| D-068 | 2026-05-25 | **H13 — File-input pickers on the CP now carry programmatic accessible names.** Two `<input type="file">` elements that previously rendered with `id` + `accept` but no label were corrected. `Profile.razor` (CP `/account/profile`) avatar picker gains a visible `<label for="avatar-input">` localised as `Profile.Avatar.Picker` (EN: "Choose avatar image file" / AR: "اختر ملف صورة الحساب"), and the existing size-hint `<p>` gets `id="avatar-input-hint"` linked from the input via `aria-describedby`. `UsersList.razor` (CP `/admin/admins` Import button) — its picker is visually hidden (triggered by JS via a `<SimfButton>` label "Import" elsewhere on the page), so `aria-label` is used instead of `<label for>`; localised as `Admin.Users.Import.Picker` (EN: "Choose Excel file to import" / AR: "اختر ملف Excel للاستيراد"). The Import-XLSX picker was worse: visually hidden and label-less, an assistive-tech traversal that surfaced it would have heard nothing meaningful. The avatar picker gets the `<label for>` + `aria-describedby` pair (the standard WCAG 1.3.1 + 3.3.2 pattern); the visually-hidden import picker can't use `<label for>` (no visible text to bind to) so it uses `aria-label` instead. | D-067 | 2026-05-25 | **H12 — Password-reset and change-password success tests now prove the credential step actually accepted the new password.** `PasswordTests.Reset_password_with_a_valid_code_sets_the_new_password` and `PasswordTests.Change_password_with_the_correct_current_password_succeeds` previously asserted only `HttpStatusCode.OK` on the post-reset / post-change sign-in. Both tests use 2FA-enabled visitors (`RegisterVerifiedVisitorAsync` / `SignInVisitorAsync` flip `TwoFactorEnabled=true`), so a 200 is the OTP challenge — NOT a full token mint. A regression that broke the password step but still emitted an OTP challenge for a wrong password would have passed silently. Both tests now read the response body as `ApiResult<SignInResponse>` and assert `Success == true`, `MfaRequired == true`, and `OtpToken` is non-empty — proving the password step did pass. No test count change (existing tests strengthened in place). Tests: **202 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 238 / 238**. | Closes Code SEV-1.4 from the 5-agent review. A status-code-only assertion on a 2FA endpoint is structurally insufficient — the endpoint returns 200 for both "password OK, here's the OTP challenge" and "password OK, here are tokens" — but it also returns 200 if a future refactor silently emits an MFA-required envelope without checking the password. The fix narrows the assertion to the actual contract: a successful password step against a 2FA-enabled visitor returns `MfaRequired=true` + non-empty `OtpToken`. The full lifecycle (OTP verify → tokens) is covered by other tests and not duplicated here; this test's job is to pin the *password*-step semantics. |
| D-066 | 2026-05-25 | **H11 — `PasswordChange.Failed` audit row now separates wrong-current from policy-rejected.** Inside `PasswordService.ChangePasswordAsync`, the post-`userManager.ChangePasswordAsync` failure branch inspects `result.Errors` for `"PasswordMismatch"`. If found, the audit row carries `ErrorCode = AuthInvalidCredentials` and `Detail = "WrongCurrent: {identityErrorCodes}"`; otherwise `ErrorCode = AuthPasswordPolicy` (new constant) and `Detail = "PolicyRejected: {identityErrorCodes}"`. The user-facing API error is unchanged — wrong-current still raises `400 / AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS` with the same bilingual message, weak-new still raises the policy `DataValidationException`. Only the audit row gains forensic detail. Regression test in `PasswordTests`: a wrong-current attempt writes `PasswordChange.Failed / AuthInvalidCredentials / Detail starts with "WrongCurrent:"`. The PolicyRejected branch is intentionally not regression-tested at the HTTP level: per D-005 the FluentValidation `StrongPassword` rules and the Identity `PasswordOptions` config enforce the exact same baseline (length 8, one digit, one letter), so no normal HTTP request reaches the Identity-layer rejection path. The audit shape is in place as defence-in-depth — it fires when Identity's policy is tightened beyond the validator, or for non-HTTP callers (seeders, admin tools) that bypass FluentValidation. Tests: **202 API** (201 + 1 H11) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **238 / 238**. | Closes Code SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The pre-H11 audit wrote `PasswordChange.Failed / AuthInvalidCredentials` for both failure modes. SOC reading the audit log could not separate brute-force-current-password attempts (where the attacker has the user's session and is iterating the current password) from a user typing weak new passwords five times in a row. The two have very different incident-response weights: the first is an active compromise signal, the second is a UX-friction signal. Adding the `AuthPasswordPolicy` error code + `WrongCurrent / PolicyRejected` Detail prefix makes the timeline reconstructable from the row alone. `AuthPasswordPolicy` lives in `ErrorCodes` because consistency demands it (every audit row's `ErrorCode` lookups to a constant), even though no API response surfaces it directly. |
| D-065 | 2026-05-25 | **H10 — `IEmailQueue.Enqueue` failures now write a distinct `Email.EnqueueFailed` audit row.** Three call sites wrapped: `PasswordService.ForgotPasswordAsync` (`PasswordReset` purpose), `RegistrationService.SignUpAsync` + `RegistrationService.ResendCodeAsync` (`EmailVerification` purpose), and `SignInService.SignInAsync` OTP-issue branch (`SignInOtp` purpose). Each `try { Enqueue(...) } catch (Exception ex) { logger.LogError; AuditAsync(EmailEnqueueFailed, Failure, detail: $"{purpose}: {ex.GetType().Name}") }`. The surrounding success audit row (e.g. `ForgotPassword.Requested = Success`) still fires — the contract that the response shape never reveals account existence is preserved — but SOC now sees the parallel `Email.EnqueueFailed` row and knows the user never actually received the message. New audit constant: `AuditEvents.EmailEnqueueFailed = "Email.EnqueueFailed"`. New test factory `ThrowingEmailQueueApiFactory` substitutes the `IEmailQueue` for an implementation that throws on every Enqueue. Two regression tests in `EmailEnqueueFailureTests`: forgot-password with throwing queue writes both Email.EnqueueFailed AND ForgotPassword.Requested(Success); sign-up with throwing queue writes Email.EnqueueFailed while the account is still created. Tests: **201 API** (199 + 2 H10) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **237 / 237**. | Closes Code SEV-1.1 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The three credential-flow paths persist a code/ticket row to the DB, then call into `IEmailQueue.Enqueue` — a separate scope outside the transaction. If the queue throws (channel closed during shutdown, OOM, downstream SMTP failure inside the worker that re-throws to the channel), the user holds a code they will never receive, and the success audit row says "ForgotPassword.Requested / Success" — masking the gap from SOC. The decision-log mention of an outbox-style guarantee under D-057 is the right long-term shape; this commit is the minimum-viable observability fix until then: catch, log, audit. Throws are NOT propagated to the caller — the always-200 contract for forgot-password (which prevents account-enumeration oracles per D-006) is preserved, and the sign-up / sign-in responses keep their existing shape. The user still gets a "request received" response; the operator sees the gap in the audit log. The same shape can later be lifted to a `WithEmailEnqueueAudit(...)` helper if a fourth call site appears. |
