Website skip-link deferred — the Website has no heavy navigation to bypass; per-page `<main>` landmarks added by H3 (D-058) on the state-banner pages suffice for that surface. Tests: **199 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 235 / 235** (no test count change — markup-only, follows the D-061 precedent for App.razor). | Closes A11y SEV-1.4 (WCAG 2.4.1 Bypass Blocks) from the 5-agent review. The Control Panel renders ~30 navigation items + the top bar's brand, language switch, theme toggle, notification bell, profile link, and sign-out form before reaching the page heading on every page. A keyboard-only user had to Tab through all of them on every navigation. The skip-link sits as the first focusable element so the first Tab press lands on it, Enter jumps focus to the `<main>` region, and the navigation is bypassed. The link is invisible (via `transform`, not `display: none`, so the focus state can transition in smoothly) and only appears on `:focus-visible` — sighted users see no visual change. `tabindex="-1"` on `<main>` is required because non-form / non-link elements aren't normally focusable via JS (`#simf-main` won't accept focus from the link target without it). Direction-aware via CSS logical properties (`inset-block-start`, `inset-inline-start`) — works in both LTR (English) and RTL (Arabic). The Website's parallel fix is deferred because (a) the Website's pages are short and lack a comparable navigation block, (b) the H3 work already added `<main>` landmarks on the state-banner pages, and (c) the bigger Website gap is the page-level `<main>` landmark missing on the auth pages, which is a separate broader fix. |
| D-063 | 2026-05-25 | **H8 — `ChangePasswordRequest.CurrentPassword` is now length-capped at 128.** `ChangePasswordRequestValidator.cs` adds `.MaximumLength(128).Bilingual("The current password is too long.", "كلمة المرور الحالية طويلة جداً.")` on `CurrentPassword`, mirroring the same ceiling `StrongPassword()` already enforces on `NewPassword`. Regression test in `PasswordTests`: posting a 129-character `CurrentPassword` returns `400 / VALIDATION_FAILED` before any PBKDF2 work. Tests: **199 API** (198 + 1 H8) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **235 / 235**. | Closes Code SEV-1.2 from the 5-agent review. Without the cap a caller could post a megabyte-sized `CurrentPassword` string; the validator's `NotEmpty()` rule would let it through, and `userManager.ChangePasswordAsync` would then run PBKDF2 over the whole payload inside a transaction — a cheap, authenticated DoS vector. The 128-char ceiling matches `StrongPassword()`'s cap on `NewPassword`; the asymmetry between the two fields was the actual bug (one field had a runtime cost ceiling, the other had none). Aligns with CLAUDE.md §7 "validator length must match storage / runtime ceilings". |
| D-062 | 2026-05-25 | **H7 — Per-email rate-limit partition guards credential endpoints across IP rotation.** Adds a second rate-limit policy `"auth-email"` to `Program.cs`'s `AddRateLimiter` that keys its `FixedWindowLimiter` on the normalised email field from the request body. A new pipeline middleware `EmailRateLimitKeyMiddleware` peeks the body of `/auth/sign-in`, `/auth/forgot-password`, and `/auth/reset-password` (the three credential-touching paths), enables `Request.EnableBuffering`, copies the body into a `MemoryStream` to parse a `JsonDocument`, scans the root object case-insensitively for an `email` string property, and stashes the normalised value (`Trim().ToLowerInvariant()`) into `HttpContext.Items["SIMF.RateLimit.Email"]`. The original request body stream is rewound to position 0 so FastEndpoints' model binder still sees the full payload. The three credential endpoints chain both policies via `Options(b => b.RequireRateLimiting("auth").RequireRateLimiting("auth-email"))`. New options: `RateLimitOptions.EmailPermitLimit` (default 5) and `EmailWindowSeconds` (default 60). New test factory `EmailRateLimitedApiFactory` tightens `EmailPermitLimit=3` while the base `SimfApiFactory` sets `EmailPermitLimit=100000` (permissive — mirrors the existing per-IP test default — so legitimate tests like `SignIn_locks_the_account_after_five_wrong_passwords` are not capped). Two regression tests in `EmailRateLimitTests`: (1) four sign-ins against the same email trip the per-email cap on the 4th; (2) a second email retains its own partition and still gets through. Tests: **198 API** (196 + 2 H7) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **234 / 234**. The null-IP `?? "unknown"` fallback is left unchanged (deferred from the original H7 plan): tightening it to `Math.Min(5, PermitLimit)` broke ASP.NET TestServer (which sets no `Connection.RemoteIpAddress` on any request) by funnelling every test request into a 5-permit bucket; with the per-email partition now bounding credential stuffing independent of IP key, the value-add of further tightening "unknown" is small. Revisit if a production signal shows misrouted no-IP traffic abusing the fallback. | Closes Security SEV-1.4 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The previous "auth" policy keyed only on the source IP — an attacker rotating proxies against `victim@example.com` was bounded per-IP (20/min default) but unbounded per-target, so the per-account lockout (5 wrong passwords) would fire after 5 attempts across N IPs, denying the legitimate user. With the new per-email partition, the same attacker is bounded to `EmailPermitLimit` attempts against the victim across the entire IP space — credential stuffing is throttled before it can DoS the account. The middleware position is `BEFORE UseRateLimiter` (between `ErrorHandlingMiddleware` and `UseRateLimiter`) because the rate limiter needs the email key set when its policy delegate runs — a stash-in-HttpContext.Items pattern fits because `Items` is the canonical per-request bag for cross-middleware signalling. The body-peek uses `EnableBuffering` + a MemoryStream copy (rather than parsing the stream directly) so the original body's position is predictable for FastEndpoints' model binder downstream. The case-insensitive `email` scan handles both `PostAsJsonAsync` (PascalCase `"Email"`) and any hand-rolled client (lowercase `"email"`) without depending on a System.Text.Json naming policy change. The `NoLimiter` fallback (when the path is unknown to the middleware) means chaining `"auth-email"` on a route that doesn't carry an email is harmless. |
| D-061 | 2026-05-25 | **H6 — Website `App.razor` `<html lang dir>` now tracks the current UI culture.** `src/Website/SIMF.Web/Components/App.razor` adds `@using System.Globalization` and renders `<html lang="@CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture.TwoLetterISOLanguageName" dir="@(CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture.TextInfo.IsRightToLeft ? "rtl" : "ltr")">`. Exactly mirrors the Control Panel's pattern (D-028). The Website already calls `UseRequestLocalization` (Program.cs:120) so `CurrentUICulture` is set from the culture cookie / Accept-Language header per request — the App.razor change is the last mile that surfaces it to the document. No new test: matches the precedent set when the same pattern landed on the CP (D-028 shipped without a bUnit assertion for the markup-only behaviour). The bigger a11y review gap (no automated coverage for the CP and Website App.razor markup contract) is noted as a follow-up. Tests: **196 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 232 / 232** (no test count change). | Closes A11y SEV-1.1 from the 5-agent review. Until this commit, every Arabic-culture Website page (`/account/profile`, `/account/notifications`, `/account/pending`, `/account/rejected`, plus the public marketing site's Blazor islands) shipped with `<html lang="en" dir="ltr">`. Screen readers (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver) announce Arabic strings with English phonemes when `lang="en"`, and the entire layout flows left-to-right while the localised text reads right-to-left — punctuation mirroring breaks, CSS logical properties (`inset-inline-start`, `text-align: start`) compute against the wrong direction, and the H3 state-banner pages' `<main>` landmark inherits the wrong direction. The CP shipped the correct pattern in D-028 (the CP localisation foundation increment); the Website regressed it. The fix is the one-line port that should have happened when the Website's localisation pipeline landed alongside the CP's. The change is markup-only — no API, contract, or behaviour change — and the build / test set is untouched. |
| D-060 | 2026-05-25 | **H5 — `OnTokenValidated` requires a non-empty `security_stamp` claim and compares it in constant time.** `JwtBearerSetup.OnTokenValidatedAsync` now does three checks instead of one: (a) the `security_stamp` claim must be present in the JWT and non-empty (otherwise audit `AccessTokenRejected` with detail `"security stamp claim missing or empty"` and 401); (b) the DB row's `SecurityStamp` must also be non-empty (defensive — no token validates against a null/empty DB stamp); (c) the comparison runs through a new `FixedTimeEquals` helper that converts both sides to UTF-8 bytes, checks length equality, and calls `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals`. Two regression tests in `JwtMiddlewareTests`: a JWT lacking the claim entirely → 401; a JWT carrying an empty-string claim → 401. Both share the existing helpers `MakeTokenWithStamp` / `MakeTokenWithoutStamp` so the existing `MakeToken` signature stays backwards-compatible. Tests: **196 API** (194 + 2 H5) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **232 / 232**. | Closes Security SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The previous check was `user.SecurityStamp != tokenStamp` against a potentially null/empty `tokenStamp`. Two ways this fails open: (1) a forged token (or a buggy custom issuer) that omits the claim ends up comparing `null` to a user whose `SecurityStamp` is also null/empty — `null != null` is `false`, the check passes, the token is honoured; (2) a token whose claim is `""` against a user row whose stamp is `""` likewise passes. In both cases sign-out, password-change, 2FA-reset, and refresh-token reuse — every revocation surface in the system — silently does nothing for the bad token. The fix is defence-in-depth: require the claim, require the DB value, and compare in constant time so a stamp prefix match is not observable through response timing. The constant-time comparison is symbolic protection more than practical (a 32-char Identity stamp has 10^48 prefix combinations; timing attacks at the network layer are infeasible for it), but `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals` is the idiomatic primitive and costs nothing. Seeded super-admin: `userManager.CreateAsync(admin, settings.TempPassword)` calls ASP.NET Identity's stamp-update internally, so the seeded row's stamp is non-null/non-empty out of the box — no seeder change needed. |
| D-059 | 2026-05-25 | **H4 — `PasswordChangeRequired` is enforced at sign-in.** `SignInService.SignInAsync` gains a single check right after the credentials succeed and `AccessFailedCount` is reset, and before the account-state and audience gates: if `user.PasswordChangeRequired == true`, the call audits `SignIn.PasswordChangeRequired` (Failure / `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`) and throws `ApiException(403)`. The user must use the existing `/auth/forgot-password` → `/auth/reset-password` flow (which clears the flag in `PasswordService.ClearChangeFlagAndEndSessionsAsync`) to mint any session. Bonus correctness fix: `SimfUser.PasswordChangeRequired` default flips from `true` to `false` — the previous default silently set the flag on every newly-created user (sign-up included), which the gate would have blocked from ever signing in; only the seeder + an operator-forced rotation should set the flag, and both already do so explicitly. New audit constant: `AuditEvents.SignInPasswordChangeRequired = "SignIn.PasswordChangeRequired"`. Two regression tests in `SignInTests`: (1) a flagged admin with the right password gets `403 / AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`; (2) the same admin once the flag is cleared completes the normal 2FA challenge. Tests: **194 API** (192 + 2 H4) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **230 / 230**. | Closes Security SEV-1.2 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The super-admin is seeded by `IdentitySeeder` with `PasswordChangeRequired = true` AND a committed temp password — the intended compensating control was "first sign-in is forced through a password change". That control was never built; the flag existed and was set but no code read it. Combined with the committed secrets (Security SEV-1.1, a separate operational issue), the seeded credential was a backdoor: the super-admin could sign in cleanly forever with the well-known password. The fix is a 12-line gate in the only place where the flag becomes relevant (the password step), wired to the audit event the SOC already understands. The default-value flip is the same bug at a different layer: a CLR default of `true` on a property whose semantic is "this is a temporary password" inverts the usual assumption (a NEW account has not yet been rotated and is not stuck with a temp password). With the default at `false`, only the seeder's explicit `= true` (and the operator-rotation path when it lands) holds it; every other create path stays naturally at `false`. The gate runs **before** the audience gate so a flagged user gets the same `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` message regardless of which surface they tried — a wrong-surface user with a flagged credential should fix the credential first; routing them away by audience would mask the real blocker. |
| D-058 | 2026-05-25 | **H3 — Notification bell + state-banner pages accessibility hardening.** Bell trigger gains `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-controls`, and `aria-expanded` keyed to the open state; its accessible name folds the unread count in (`"Notifications, 3 unread"`) so a screen-reader user gets the same information the visual badge gives. New `UnreadSuffixLabel` parameter (defaults `"unread"`) — wired through CP `Strings.resx` (`unread`) and `Strings.ar.resx` (`غير مقروءة`). The dropdown becomes a focus-managed dialog: receives focus on open via `ElementReference.FocusAsync` in `OnAfterRenderAsync`, closes on Escape (handled on both trigger and dropdown), and closes on outside-click via a transparent fixed-position backdrop sibling at `z-index: 49` (the dropdown sits at 50). Inner clicks `stopPropagation` so the dropdown does not eat itself. Four state-banner pages (CP `/auth/pending` + `/auth/rejected`; Website `/account/pending` + `/account/rejected`) replace their top `<div>` wrapper with `<main aria-labelledby="...">` referencing the `<h1>` — gives assistive tech a landmark to jump to on these full-page state messages. Tests: **192 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 228 / 228** (no test count change — H3 is markup + JS-runtime focus). | Closes SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The earlier P12 bell shipped with `role="dialog"` on the dropdown and an `aria-label` on the trigger, but a keyboard-only user could not tell whether the popup was open, could not get focus into the popup, could not close it without clicking the bell again, and the unread badge was `aria-hidden` with no spoken substitute. The fix uses native primitives (HTML attributes, `KeyboardEventArgs`, `ElementReference.FocusAsync`) — no MudBlazor or third-party widget — matching D-021's "Simf* primitives are semantic HTML" stance. Click-outside is a transparent backdrop rather than a JS `document` listener because the backdrop participates in Blazor Server's event pipeline, leaves no listener to dispose of, and disappears with the dropdown automatically. The `<main>` landmark is a single-element change on each of the four banner pages — no layout impact — and gives the pages a screen-reader landmark they were missing (the banner is the page's primary content; without `<main>` the navigation-skip target was the body root). The `UnreadSuffixLabel` parameter is added with a default value so existing CP wiring stays working before the bilingual key is bound; the Website does not yet render the bell, so no Website wiring is needed here. |
| D-057 | 2026-05-25 | **H2 — `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` first-save auto-transition is now transactional, and a state flip revokes every live refresh token.** Three writes — the profile rows, the `EmailVerified → PendingApproval` state change on `SimfUser`, and `IRefreshTokenRepository.RevokeAllForUserAsync` — now commit through one `ITransactionRunner.ExecuteAsync` scope. `INotificationDispatcher` calls (the visitor-thanks + every-admin-pending-visitor messages) move **after** the commit, so an email-send failure does not roll back the DB. Regression test in `UserProfileTests`: an `EmailVerified` visitor signs in (mints a live refresh token), upserts a valid profile, and the test asserts `AccountState == PendingApproval`, `StateChangedAt` is set, and zero refresh tokens remain unrevoked. Tests: **192 API** (191 + 1) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **228 / 228**. | Closes SEV-1.2 from the 5-agent review. P13 (D-054) wired the auto-transition outside the profile save — a crash between `dbContext.SaveChangesAsync` and `userManager.UpdateAsync(user)` would leave the user as `EmailVerified` (the UI has no path back to the profile form because the user thinks they already submitted), and the existing access + refresh tokens would keep encoding `account_state=EmailVerified` for their full lifetime, bypassing the Pending banner P11 added. Wrapping the three writes in the existing `ITransactionRunner` matches the established `RegistrationService` / `PasswordService` pattern (sign-up + first verification code committed together — D-002 era). Notifications stay outside the transaction deliberately: an email enqueue or in-app row write is a side-effect on a different scope (`IEmailQueue`, `INotificationService`) and a transient failure there should not roll back a settled state change — re-trying notifications is straightforward; un-settling an account state is not. The trade-off: a dispatch failure means the first-time visitor message is missed for that user, accepted because the state is correct and an admin can re-notify by re-rejecting/re-approving. An outbox-style guarantee for the first dispatch is a separate hardening item, recorded here so it is not lost. |
| D-056 | 2026-05-25 | **H1 — `RequireApprovedAccount` policy sweep across every admin endpoint.** Adds the existing `RequireApprovedAccount` policy alongside `AdministratorOnly` on all 15 endpoint classes under `src/Backend/SIMF.Api/Endpoints/Admin/`, including `Logs/LogsEndpoints.cs`. The policy gate keys off the `account_state=Approved` JWT claim added by P10 (D-051). Regression test in `AdminApprovalTests`: a Pending administrator signs in successfully (P10 allows authentication) but receives `403 Forbidden` when calling `POST /admin/admins/list`. Tests: **192 API** (191 + 1 H1 regression) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **228 / 228**. | Closes SEV-1.1 from the 5-agent sprint review. P10 — by design — lets `PendingApproval` and `Rejected` accounts mint a JWT so they can reach `/auth/pending` and `/auth/rejected`. Before H1 that same JWT was sufficient for every admin endpoint, because the only gate was the `Administrator` role; a Pending or Rejected admin held the role and could mutate user accounts, run bulk deletes, export the user list, and rotate other admins' 2FA. Layering `RequireApprovedAccount` keeps the role check (still needed — `Administrator` is the *who*) and adds the state check (the *whether* — only an Approved admin acts). The fix is a one-liner per endpoint (`Policies(nameof(AdministratorOnly), nameof(RequireApprovedAccount));`), not a middleware swap, so the diff is auditable file-by-file. The same policy is also added to other admin surfaces under `/account/*` separately if the review surfaces them; this commit scope is the API layer. |
| D-055 | 2026-05-24 | **CP page split per the three-UserType model (P7e).** Closes the P7 chain — P7a-d shipped the API + contracts; P7e finishes the rename on the CP UI. **Routes:** `/admin/staff` → `/admin/admins`; `/admin/staff/pending` → `/admin/admins/pending`; `/admin/staff/new` → `/admin/admins/new`. **New pages:** `OthersList.razor` at `/admin/others`, `PendingOthers.razor` at `/admin/others/pending`, `CreateOther.razor` at `/admin/others/new` (with a mandatory ProfileType picker sourced from `/admin/profile-types?userType=Other`). VisitorsList + CreateVisitor keep their routes. **Nav drift cleanup:** CpNavigation's System group gains 6 user-management entries (Admins / Pending admins / Others / Pending others / Visitors / Pending visitors) — replacing the 4 old "Staff" entries. **Proxy paths inside the Razor pages** updated from `/account/api/admin/staff/*` → `/account/api/admin/admins/*` (P7c's API renames went unfollowed in the CP UI; sed-bulk fix here). **Tests:** `CpNavigationTests` gains 6 InlineData rows asserting the new routes resolve + a regression test that the legacy `/admin/staff` route is no longer in the nav. Full suite: **226 / 226** (190 API + 13 client + 18 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | The renames are mechanical — the routes were already correct on the API side (P7c) and the contracts (P7d); P7e brings the CP UI in line. Keeping the existing resource keys (e.g. `Admin.Users.Title`) rather than re-keying everything is a deliberate trade-off: the strings still describe the right thing (an "admin" is a user), and re-keying 30+ string lookups across multiple pages would be churn for no user-visible benefit. The new pages are intentionally minimal — list + create + pending — mirroring the existing Admin / Visitor pair. The CreateOther page is the only one with extra UI (ProfileType picker), because P7d's API requires it. Splitting into three pairs (rather than one combined "Users" grid with a type filter) matches the three-UserType model literally — operators reading the nav see what the system actually models, no implicit filtering. |
| D-054 | 2026-05-24 | **Lifecycle notification + email wire-up + auto-transition (P13).** Wires the P12 (D-053) dispatcher into every account-lifecycle event. **State auto-transition:** when a visitor in `EmailVerified` state submits their profile for the first time, `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` transitions them to `PendingApproval` automatically. The transition is the moment the whole notification chain fires. **Triggers wired:** (1) profile-submitted → in-app + email to the visitor; (2) admin-pending-visitor → in-app + email to every Approved admin; (3) account-approved → in-app + email to the subject with the QR id; (4) account-rejected → in-app + email to the subject with the bilingual reason; (5) admin-2FA-reset → in-app + email to the subject with the reason. **Email templates** ship as inline bilingual HTML strings in `NotificationEmailTemplates` — token substitution is dumb `string.Replace` with `WebUtility.HtmlEncode` on every value. One template per kind × two cultures (EN/AR). A future re-skin (header logo, footer) swaps the implementation behind `Render` without changing call sites. **3 new tests** in `NotificationLifecycleTests`: first profile-submit transitions to PendingApproval + dispatches to visitor + every admin; admin-approve dispatches `Account.Approved` with QR id; admin-reject dispatches `Account.Rejected` with reason. Full suite: **219 / 219** (190 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | The auto-transition closes the only gap in the account-state machine: today self-registered visitors stopped at `EmailVerified` with no path to `PendingApproval`. Triggering on first profile-save (not on every save) means a visitor editing their profile later does not re-fire the admin notification storm. The bilingual-inline-strings template approach was chosen over file-on-disk templates because it keeps the email-template engine out of the deployment surface (no file-copy step, no path resolution) and reads cleanly in code review. The `PreRenderedEmailHtml` field on `NotificationRequest` is the seam: P12 already exposed it, P13 fills it; a future renderer swap (e.g. RazorLight for an HTML re-skin) plugs in here. Notifying every admin (rather than a single "ops" address) avoids picking a contact person — anyone with the Administrator role sees the new pending visitor in their bell. The English-template default (not picking the recipient's culture per row) is deliberate v1 — most recipients are bilingual SIMF staff and the in-app row carries both languages; a per-user culture preference is a P14 follow-up if needed. |
| D-053 | 2026-05-24 | **In-app notifications foundation (P12).** New `Notification` entity on the Identity DbContext + EF migration `AddNotifications`. Bilingual title + body in the row so the page reads by current culture without a second lookup. Severity = `Info / Success / Warning / Error`. Two indexes: `(UserId, CreatedAt DESC)` for the page + bell dropdown, `(UserId, ReadAt)` for the unread-count poll. **Dispatcher** `INotificationDispatcher.DispatchAsync(NotificationRequest)` — always writes the DB row, optionally queues an email through the existing `IEmailQueue` (P13 wires the email templates). **Service** `INotificationService` — actor-scoped reads/mutations (list paged, unread-count, mark-read idempotent, mark-all-read via `ExecuteUpdateAsync`, delete idempotent via `ExecuteDeleteAsync`). **API** — five endpoints under `/api/v1/account/notifications/*`. **Client** — `SimfAccountClient` gains five typed methods; both apps' `/account/api/notifications/*` proxy forwards same-origin so a guest token works without role checks. **UI** — `SimfNotificationBell` primitive (bell icon + unread badge + dropdown of latest 5 unread + "Mark all read" + "View all", polls unread-count every 60 s) mounted on the CP top bar. New `/account/notifications` page on both apps via `SimfDataGrid`. Website's `simf-account.js` gains a `deleteJson` helper to match CP. **No callers yet** — P13 wires every lifecycle trigger. **6 tests** in `NotificationTests` — list-only-actor's-rows + newest-first ordering; unread-count excludes read rows; mark-read idempotent + flips ReadAt; mark-all-read clears every unread; delete idempotent; dispatcher writes a row. Full suite: **216 / 216** (187 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | Notifications live on the Identity context because every row keys to a user. Bilingual columns (rather than a join to a translation table) match the pattern P9 / D-030 set — every user-facing string ships EN + AR in the row so culture-switching never round-trips. The dispatcher's `SendEmail : bool` lets the same call write the in-app row without forcing the email channel. The bell polls `unread-count` (cheapest read) rather than the full list, so 100 active CP sessions are 100 row-count queries per minute — fine on the indexed hot path. The page lives at `/account/notifications` on both apps, matching the "account-area lives under /account" convention. `SimfDataGrid` reuse keeps the page accessible + paged for free. The component lives in `SIMF.Components.Controls` rather than `SIMF.Components.Forms` because it's a chrome element, not a form input. Shipping the foundation without callers (the whole P12) keeps the diff reviewable; P13 will turn it on one trigger at a time. |
| D-052 | 2026-05-24 | **Guest-only routing + Pending/Rejected pages (P11).** Closes the loop opened by P10 (D-051) — non-approved users now actually land somewhere useful instead of getting tokens with no UI to consume them. **Cookie surfaces the state:** the `/auth/complete` endpoint on both CP and Website decodes the JWT's `account_state` + `user_type` claims and writes them onto the cookie principal, alongside the bilingual rejection reason (read from the API's sign-in response — the JWT itself does not carry it). The sign-in ticket store gains an `AccountStateInfo?` field so the reason rides the hand-off. **Pages:** four new `Authorize`d pages — CP `/auth/pending` + `/auth/rejected` and Website `/account/pending` + `/account/rejected` — each reads the cookie claims (no API call), shows a clear bilingual title + supporting text + sign-out button, and shows the rejection reason in the visitor's culture (Arabic first when culture is `ar`, English fallback). The pages cross-redirect among themselves and to the dashboard if the cookie state drifts. **Layout-level guard:** the CP `CpShellLayout` checks `account_state` in `OnInitializedAsync` and routes non-Approved users to the matching state-banner page before any module content renders. The Website's `Account/UserProfile.razor` does the same. The state-banner pages use `MainLayout` (not `CpShellLayout`) so they never trigger the guard themselves. **API:** a new `RequireApprovedAccount` named policy is registered alongside `AdministratorOnly` (gates by `account_state == "Approved"`); **not** applied to any endpoint in this commit — the client-side routing is the primary gate and a follow-up sweep will opt sensitive endpoints into the policy explicitly. **Direct routing on `/auth/complete`:** rather than relying on the layout guard to redirect after the dashboard half-renders, the completion endpoint itself returns the right redirect target based on the just-decoded state. This avoids a flash of the dashboard. **Tests:** +2 in `SignInTests` (JWT for pending admin carries `account_state=PendingApproval` + `user_type=Admin`; JWT for rejected user carries `account_state=Rejected`); rewrote `SignInTicketStoreTests` to match the new payload shape + added an `AccountStateInfo round-trips` test. New `System.IdentityModel.Tokens.Jwt 8.2.1` package reference on `SIMF.Web` (the CP already had it transitively). Full suite: **210 / 210** pass (181 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | Reading the JWT once at `/auth/complete` and copying the claims onto the cookie is the right shape: every protected Blazor page can branch on the cookie identity without ever decoding the JWT (the cookie scheme already runs on every request). Writing the rejection reason onto the cookie (vs an on-demand API call from the rejected page) keeps the state-banner pages dependency-free — they don't need an HTTP round-trip just to render a sentence. The bilingual reason rides as two separate claims (`rejection_reason` + `rejection_reason_ar`) so the page picks by culture without re-fetching, matching D-030 (every API surface carries EN + AR). The layout guard catches the rare drift case (admin rejected the user in another tab while the visitor was on `/account/profile`) without forcing every page to re-check; this is the same shape as the existing `[Authorize]` default-deny in CP `_Imports.razor` — one place to enforce, every page inherits. The direct redirect on `/auth/complete` avoids the "flash of the dashboard" UX glitch that the layout guard alone would produce (it runs after `Body` first renders). The new `RequireApprovedAccount` policy is shipped but unused on purpose: the API-side hardening needs a careful sweep of every endpoint to decide which ones a guest CAN reach (`/auth/sign-out`, P12 notifications, …) — that is the next focused commit, not the right place to do it during P11. The `MainLayout` choice for the state-banner pages prevents the guard-redirect loop without needing special-case logic in the guard itself — clean by construction. The ticket-payload refactor is the simplest hand-off shape that survives both no-2FA (where `AccountStateInfo` is on the response) and 2FA (where the JWT carries `account_state` and the verify endpoints don't return state info — the JWT decode at `/auth/complete` covers it). |
| D-051 | 2026-05-24 | **Non-approved users sign in as guests — rejection reason persists, JWT carries `account_state`, `SignInResponse` carries `AccountStateInfo` (P10).** Sign-in for `PendingApproval` and `Rejected` accounts no longer 403s. The user gets tokens + a structured `AccountStateInfo { State, RejectionReason, RejectionReasonArabic, StateChangedAt }` on the password-step response, and the JWT itself carries two new claims — `account_state` (Registered / EmailVerified / PendingApproval / Approved / Rejected / Disabled) and `user_type` (Admin / Other / Visitor). `Disabled` stays a hard 403 (operator's "this account is dead" lever). `Registered` stays "verify your email first". The new claims travel in every access token so the P11 authorization handler can route a non-approved user to the pending / rejected page without an extra round-trip; the client switches surfaces (CP vs App) by `user_type` alone. **Schema:** four new nullable columns on `AspNetUsers` — `RejectionReason : nvarchar(500)`, `RejectionReasonArabic : nvarchar(500)`, `StateChangedAt : datetimeoffset`, `StateChangedByUserId : uniqueidentifier`. Composite index `IX_AspNetUsers_AccountState_StateChangedAt` supports the "recently rejected / approved" admin query. Migration `AddAccountStateMetadata` is additive and back-fills `StateChangedAt = CreatedAt` for every existing row so the new index has sane sort keys from day one. **Behaviour:** `AdminAccountService.RejectAsync` now writes the reason on the user row (was audit-only before — EN-only admin input mirrors to `RejectionReasonArabic` as a graceful fallback per R1 default; a future bilingual reject form may diverge the two). `AdminAccountService.ApproveAsync` clears both reason fields on the reconsider path so a once-rejected user does not see stale text after re-approval. **Both** state-change paths now call `IRefreshTokenRepository.RevokeAllForUserAsync` so the subject's next API call gets a fresh access token with the new `account_state` claim — without this, a just-rejected user could keep using a stale token for ≤ 15 minutes. New audit event `SignIn.AsGuest` fires when a `PendingApproval` / `Rejected` user signs in (one per sign-in) so SOC can spot non-approved sign-ins separately from `SignIn.Succeeded`. **Tests:** **3 new in `SignInTests`** (pending → AccountStateInfo present + audit; rejected → bilingual reason + audit; approved → AccountStateInfo null) and **+1 / -1 in `AdminApprovalTests`** (rewrote the old "reject blocks sign-in" assertion as "reject persists reason + metadata on user row"; added "approve-after-reject clears the reason"). The old `SignIn_for_a_pending_staff_returns_403_AUTH_ACCOUNT_NOT_APPROVED` test is **replaced** because its premise no longer holds. Full suite: **207 / 207** (179 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). **Out of scope (lives in P11):** the `RequireApprovedAccount` authorization handler that gates every endpoint except the state-banner pages + notifications + sign-out; the Website / CP `PendingApproval.razor` + `Rejected.razor` pages; the sign-in client branch that reads `account_state` and routes. **Until P11 ships, P10 alone changes the API contract** — a non-approved user gets tokens but the existing client UI does not yet know what to do with them. | The user's instruction was direct ("can login before approved and after reject but in jwt token must show isnot approved so will able to show notification on app and login as guest only"). Two facets: tokens issued + JWT carrying the state so the front-end can route the user to a state-locked page. Persisting the reason on the user row (vs the audit-only P4 shape) is the only way for the user's own sign-in response to carry it — without a second DB call. Splitting the audit event (`SignIn.AsGuest` vs `SignIn.Succeeded`) keeps SOC dashboards clean: a guest sign-in is not a failure (the rejection already happened) but it is a distinct outcome worth counting. The refresh-token revoke on every state change is the security gate — without it the relaxed sign-in would let a freshly-rejected session keep working until token expiry. The R1 default (EN-only admin input mirrored to Arabic) is the cheaper UX today; the schema already supports independent bilingual reasons for the day the admin form upgrades. The bilingual surface mirrors D-030 (every API error carries EN + AR) so the visitor on a rejected account sees the reason in their language. Keeping `Disabled` as a hard 403 preserves the operator's emergency lever — relaxing it would defeat the whole point of the state. The composite index is a no-cost forward-looking add: any future "show me rejections in the last 30 days" admin view hits it directly. Surfacing `AccountStateInfo` only on the password-step response (and not on the TOTP/OTP/RecoveryCode verify responses) keeps the change surface tight — the `account_state` JWT claim is the source of truth for any later sign-in step or any cached token, so the verify endpoints don't need a separate payload. The replaced test is **the right test to remove**: the old P4 behaviour is gone; pretending otherwise would silently let the new contract drift. |

The list is **admin-managed in the CP** — no hardcoded seed: a fresh install starts with an empty `Interests` table and the visitor page shows a "list not published yet" notice until an admin creates the first row. **Domain:** new `Interest { Id, Name, NameArabic, DisplayOrder, IsActive, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt? }`; `UserProfile.Interests : ICollection<Interest>` via EF skip-nav. **Schema** (additive — migration `AddInterests`): `Interests` table (PK Id, Name unique, composite index `(IsActive, DisplayOrder)`), `UserProfileInterests` join (composite PK + both FKs `OnDelete Cascade`). **API:** visitor-side `GET /api/v1/account/interests` (active only, ordered); admin-side CRUD under `/api/v1/admin/interests` — `POST list` (paged grid via `GridQuery`), `GET {id}`, `POST` (create, 409 on duplicate Name), `PUT {id}` (update + activate/deactivate), `DELETE {id}` (soft-delete, idempotent). **Contract:** `UpsertUserProfileRequest.InterestIds : IList<Guid>` and `UserProfileResponse.InterestIds` — the validator enforces count (1-10) + distinct ids; the service re-checks every id exists and is active (defence in depth, fail with `INTEREST_INVALID`). **CP:** `/admin/interests` (`SimfDataGrid` with per-row Edit + Deactivate), `/admin/interests/new`, `/admin/interests/{id}/edit`; one new nav entry under "System". **Website:** the profile page renders a new `SimfCheckList<TItem>` primitive with the active interests, inline layout, bilingual labels (Arabic when culture is `ar`); when the lookup is empty the page surfaces "the list has not been published yet". **Client:** `SimfAdminClient` gains five new methods (`ListInterestsAsync`, `GetInterestAsync`, `CreateInterestAsync`, `UpdateInterestAsync`, `DeactivateInterestAsync`); `SimfAccountClient` gains `GetActiveInterestsAsync`. **Audit events:** `Interest.Created`, `Interest.Updated`, `Interest.Deactivated` (one row per mutation). **Error codes:** `INTEREST_INVALID`, `INTEREST_NOT_FOUND`, `INTEREST_NAME_DUPLICATE`. **Resource strings:** ~45 new (≈22 EN + AR pairs each) covering CP page chrome + Website section. **Tests:** **new `InterestTests`** (6 — admin CRUD round-trip, duplicate-name 409, update + deactivate, visitor list filtered by IsActive, non-admin 403, 404 on unknown id); **`UserProfileTests`** gains 5 (min-1, max-10, unknown id, deactivated id, round-trip with diff-and-save) and refactors `ValidSaudiRequest` to async + seed an interest per call. Full suite: **204 / 204** (176 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). **Latent fix folded in:** the P8 sed renaming `"/profile"` → `"/user-profile"` missed three Website-proxy paths (`/profile/countries`, `/profile/id-image` POST + GET) because their literal strings included a trailing segment; this commit corrects them. | The user's reasoning landed plainly: interests are a many-to-many between visitors and a controlled lookup; the lookup must be admin-CRUD-managed (no hardcoded list) so a non-developer can curate the categories per event. The 1-10 cap is the user's call ("min 1 max 10") — it balances forced commitment (one is the minimum signal) against picker overload. The auto-generated join table is correct for v1: no per-pick metadata is recorded today, and `EF` skip-nav makes the API ergonomic. The diff-and-save in `Upsert` (`toRemove = existing \ requested`, `toAdd = requested \ existing`) avoids the EF anti-pattern of clear-then-re-add, which generates `DELETE` + `INSERT` for unchanged rows and rewrites `CreatedAt`-equivalents on the join. Validation in two places — validator (count) + service (every id active) — is defence in depth; a malicious client that bypasses FluentValidation still cannot persist garbage. The "empty seed" choice keeps the operator in control: no out-of-the-box opinions about what interests SIMF should have. The deactivation path (`IsActive = false`) keeps existing user picks intact while removing the row from the visitor picker — operators can resurrect a row without rebinding any user. Splitting the visitor surface (`/account/interests` GET) from the admin surface (`/admin/interests` CRUD) lets the proxy enforce role at the boundary cleanly. The latent P8 proxy paths were caught only because P9's interest-section needed a working profile fetch; flagged + fixed honestly in the same commit since the diff is one logical area. |
| D-049 | 2026-05-24 | **Single `UserProfile` entity; `ProfileTypeId` moved off `SimfUser`; legacy `VisitorType` string dropped (P8).** Architectural simplification: the per-account form data lives on **one** entity — `UserProfile` — that covers every non-Admin user (Visitor and Other). Admin-typed users do not carry a profile. The `ProfileTypes` FK moves from `SimfUser.ProfileTypeId` to `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId`, because the lookup row is a property of the profile (the tier or partner-kind), not of the user identity. The legacy `VisitorType` string column (`"Visitor" / "Exhibitor" / "Press"`) is deleted — the same idea now flows through the `ProfileTypeId` FK assigned by an admin, not a free-text self-claim. **The change is one commit** because a partial rename would leave the schema in a broken intermediate state. **Schema renames:** table `VisitorProfiles` → `UserProfiles` (sp_rename, preserves data); PK / unique index renamed in lock-step; `UserProfiles.ProfileTypeId : Guid?` added with FK to `ProfileTypes` (Restrict on delete); `VisitorProfiles.VisitorType` column dropped; `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` column + index + FK dropped. **Back-fill:** the migration UPDATEs `UserProfiles.ProfileTypeId` from `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` for any user that already had a profile row, and INSERTs a minimal stub `UserProfile` row (Arabic/English name `''`, nationality `SA`, IsSaudi 0) for any non-Admin user that had a `ProfileTypeId` on the user row but no profile yet — so the P7a/P7d admin-pick is preserved through the cut-over. **API surface renames:** `/api/v1/account/visitor-profile/*` → `/api/v1/account/user-profile/*` (the existing `/account/profile` GET stays — it's the lightweight identity-card endpoint, kept separate from the rich registration form). **Code-symbol renames:** `VisitorProfile` → `UserProfile` (domain + contracts + page + tests + service + config); `IVisitorProfileService` → `IUserProfileService`; `IVisitorIdStorage` / `EncryptedVisitorIdStorage` → `IUserIdDocumentStorage` / `EncryptedUserIdDocumentStorage`; `VisitorIdImage` → `UserIdDocumentImage`; `VisitorIdRead` → `UserIdDocumentRead`. **Config keys:** `Storage:VisitorIdBase` → `Storage:UserIdDocumentBase`; `Storage:VisitorIdEncryptionKey` → `Storage:UserIdDocumentEncryptionKey` — operators must update `appsettings*.json` **and** the env-var overrides. **Audit events:** `VisitorProfile.{Saved,IdImageUploaded,IdImageRejected}` → `UserProfile.{Saved,IdImageUploaded,IdImageRejected}`. **Error codes:** `VISITOR_NATIONALITY_UNKNOWN` → `PROFILE_NATIONALITY_UNKNOWN`; the `VISITOR_ID_IMAGE_*` wire codes are deliberately **kept** (the symbol still says `Visitor` but the wire string is unchanged so an external consumer never breaks). **Stub-on-create:** `AdminAccountService.CreateAccountAsync` (P7d) now drops a stub `UserProfile` row when an admin picks a ProfileTypeId for an Other/Visitor user, so the FK lands atomically with the user. **Resource strings:** EN + AR `Visitor.Profile.*` keys re-keyed to `Account.Profile.*`; the four `Visitor.Profile.Type{,.Visitor,.Exhibitor,.Press}` keys are removed entirely. **Tests:** `VisitorProfileTests` → `UserProfileTests`; the `VisitorType = "Visitor"` line in the `ValidSaudiRequest` helper is gone. Full suite green: 193 / 193 (165 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | The user's reasoning was direct ("it's a profile type not a user type, why put it in the user?"). One profile entity for every non-Admin user keeps the model honest: there is one row per profile, one place to look, one FK per concern. The single-commit shape is correct here because the schema is one connected change — splitting would leave one commit on a schema that compiles but is internally inconsistent (table renamed but column not yet dropped, etc.). The data-preserving migration (sp_rename + back-fill UPDATE + back-fill INSERT) means P7d admins who already picked a ProfileTypeId for an Other / Visitor user keep their assignment across the cut-over — no operator follow-up needed. The route split (`/account/profile` for identity card vs `/account/user-profile` for the registration form) sidesteps a name collision with the existing CP profile endpoint; the two surfaces serve different consumers (CP top-bar vs Website registration page). The `VISITOR_ID_IMAGE_*` wire codes stay because they describe the user's ID document (not the user type) and any external mapping must continue to work; we will rename the C# symbols in a later sweep when the external surface is verified to have no consumers. |
| D-048 | 2026-05-24 | **Three hardcoded `UserType`s + dynamic `ProfileTypes` lookup (P7a–P7d).** Every `SimfUser` carries one of three hardcoded user-kinds: `Admin` (CP only, the **only** type that carries RBAC roles), `Other` (Flutter app — Staff / Exhibitor / Sponsor / Media / …), and `Visitor` (App + Website — VVIP / VIP / Gold / …). Subtype-within-type is admin-managed via a `ProfileTypes` table — `{ Id, Name, NameArabic, PageColor, UserType, IsActive }` with a discriminator on `UserType` so a row scopes itself to a single user-kind. **Sign-in audience gate (P7b):** the `cp / web / app` audience now keys off `UserType` (Admin → CP only; Other / Visitor → App / Website) rather than "has any RBAC role"; the `TeamMember` policy was retired (RBAC sub-grades for Admin land later). **Admin endpoints (P7c–P7d):** create-Admin / create-Other / create-Visitor split into three URLs (`/admin/admins`, `/admin/others`, `/admin/visitors`), each Administrator-only, with `ProfileTypeId` validated against the lookup + the user-type discriminator before the user row is created. **Migration `AddUserTypeAndProfileType`:** adds `AspNetUsers.UserType` (string, default `Visitor`); back-fills existing Administrator-role holders to `Admin`; creates the `ProfileTypes` table with the composite `IX_ProfileTypes_UserType_IsActive` index. **Initial seed:** `IdentitySeeder` plants two starter rows — `Visitor – General` and `Other – Staff` — both active. **D-049 supersedes** the `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` part of this decision — the FK moves to `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId`. | The user clarified the model mid-session ("ONLY THIS USER TYPE [Admin] CAN DO ANY THING; the other will work in APP").
